\eta^{\pi}_{r^{1}}
and
\eta^{\pi}_{r^{2}}

have finite supports, since in a tabular MDP with deterministic reward the
number of trajectories is finite, and, thus, also the number of corresponding
returns must be finite. We denote by supp
(\cdot)
the support of a distribution.
At (3) we use the definition of return distributions, at (4) we use the
definition of Lipschitz functions, at (5) we denote by

(s_{1},a_{1},\dotsc,s_{H},a_{H})
the state-action trajectory
\omega\in\Omega_{H+1}
,
and we use the definition of operators
G(\cdot;r^{1})
and
G(\cdot;r^{2})
.
∎

Lemma C.2
.

Let
\mathcal{M}_{r}
be any MDP and let
\pi\in\Pi^{\text{NM}}
be any expert’s policy.
Let
\pi_{r}\in\Pi(r)
be the policy defined as in Eq. (
6
) for expert’s policy
\pi
.
Then, for all
h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket
,
s\in\mathcal{S}
and
g\in[0,h-1]
, it holds that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{r}}\Big(\sum\limits_{h^{\prime}=1}^{h-1}r_{h^{\prime}}(s_{h^{\prime}},a_{h^{\prime}})=g\wedge s_{h}=s\Big)=\mathbb{P}^{\pi}\Big(\sum\limits_{h^{\prime}=1}^{h-1}r_{h^{\prime}}(s_{h^{\prime}},a_{h^{\prime}})=g\wedge s_{h}=s\Big).

Proof.

For simplicity, let us denote by
G_{h}
the
random
return at time step

h
under reward
r
. Formally:

\displaystyle G_{h}\coloneqq\sum\limits_{h^{\prime}=1}^{h-1}r_{h^{\prime}}(s_{h^{\prime}},a_{h^{\prime}})\quad\forall h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket.

In this way, the claim of the theorem can be easily rewritten as:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{r}}\Big(G_{h}=g\wedge s_{h}=s\Big)=\mathbb{P}^{\pi}\Big(G_{h}=g\wedge s_{h}=s\Big).

We prove the result by induction.
Let us begin with the base case:
h=1
. For all
s\in\mathcal{S}
and
g\in\{0\}
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{r}}\Big(G_{1}=g\wedge s_{1}=s\Big)=\mathds{1}\{g=0\}\mathds{1}\{s=s_{0}\}=\mathbb{P}^{\pi}\Big(G_{1}=g\wedge s_{1}=s\Big),

where we noticed that, for
h=1
, no action is taken yet.
Now, let us consider any stage
h\in\{2,3,\dotsc,H\}
, and let us make the
induction hypothesis that, for all
h^{\prime}\in\llbracket h-1\rrbracket
, for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
and

g\in[0,h^{\prime}-1]
, it holds that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{r}}\Big(G_{h^{\prime}}=g\wedge s_{h^{\prime}}=s\Big)=\mathbb{P}^{\pi}\Big(G_{h^{\prime}}=g\wedge s_{h^{\prime}}=s\Big).

Then, for any
s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}
and
g^{\prime}\in[0,h-1]
, we can write:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{r}}(G_{h}=g^{\prime}\wedge s_{h}=s^{\prime})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(1)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1},(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
G(\omega;r)+r_{h-1}(s,a)=g^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{r}}(\omega_{h-1}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s\wedge a_{h-1}=a\wedge s_{h}=s^{\prime})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(2)}}}{=}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1}:\\
G(\omega;r)=g\end{subarray}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}(s,a)=g^{\prime}-g\end{subarray}}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{r}}(\omega_{h-1}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s\wedge a_{h-1}=a\wedge s_{h}=s^{\prime})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(3)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1}:\\
G(\omega;r)=g\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}(s,a)=g^{\prime}-g\end{subarray}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{r}}(\omega_{h-1}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\cdot\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{r}}(a_{h-1}=a|\omega,s)\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{r}}(s_{h}=s^{\prime}|\omega,s,a)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(4)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1}:\\
G(\omega;r)=g\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}(s,a)=g^{\prime}-g\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{r}}(\omega_{h-1}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{r}}(a_{h-1}=a|\omega,s){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(5)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1}:\\
G(\omega;r)=g\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}(s,a)=g^{\prime}-g\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{r}}(\omega_{h-1}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pi_{r}(a|\omega,s)}p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(6)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h-1}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}(s,a)=g^{\prime}-g\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1}:\\
G(\omega;r)=g\end{subarray}}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{r}}(\omega_{h-1}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\pi_{r}(a|\omega,s)p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(7)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}(s,a)=g^{\prime}-g\end{subarray}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pi_{r}(a|g,s)p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1}:\\
G(\omega;r)=g\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{r}}(\omega_{h-1}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}(s,a)=g^{\prime}-g\end{subarray}}\pi_{r}(a|g,s)p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{r}}(G_{h-1}=g\wedge s_{h-1}=s)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(8)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}(s,a)=g^{\prime}-g\end{subarray}}\pi_{r}(a|g,s)p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}}(G_{h-1}=g\wedge s_{h-1}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(9)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}(s,a)=g^{\prime}-g\end{subarray}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\frac{\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h-1}=g\wedge s_{h-1}=s\wedge a_{h-1}=a)}{\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h-1}=g\wedge s_{h-1}=s)}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h-1}=g\wedge s_{h-1}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}(s,a)=g^{\prime}-g\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h-1}=g\wedge s_{h-1}=s\wedge a_{h-1}=a)p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}(s,a)=g^{\prime}-g\end{subarray}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h-1}=g\wedge s_{h-1}=s\wedge a_{h-1}=a\wedge s_{h}=s^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h-1}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h-1}=g\wedge r_{h-1}(s_{h-1},a_{h-1})=g^{\prime}-g\wedge s_{h}=s^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h}=g^{\prime}\wedge s_{h}=s^{\prime}),

where at (1) we use symbol
\omega_{h^{\prime\prime}}
to denote the random trajectory long

h^{\prime\prime}
stages, i.e., whose realizations belong to
\Omega_{h^{\prime\prime}}
, for any

h^{\prime\prime}\in\llbracket H\rrbracket
.
At (2) we define symbols
\mathcal{G}_{r,h}\coloneqq\{g\in[0,h-1]\,|\,\exists\omega\in\Omega_{h}:\,G(\omega;r)=g\}
for any
h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket
, denoting the set of
possible values of cumulative reward obtainable at stage
h
, and we sum over
all such values (note that they are finite in tabular MDPs with deterministic
rewards), at (3) we use the chain rule of conditional probabilities, at (4) we
use the Markovianity of the environment, at (5) we note that

\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{r}}(a_{h-1}=a|\omega,s)
actually is
\pi_{r}(a|\omega,s)
, at (6) we
exchange the two summations, at (7) we recognize that, by definition,

\pi_{r}(a|\omega,s)
takes on the same value for all the trajectories
\omega

with the same value of return, and thus we can bring this quantity outside the
summation over the
\omega
. We use symbol
\pi_{r}(a|g,s)
to denote this fact
for brevity. We do the same also for
p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)
.
At (8) we use the induction hypothesis, at (9) we replace
\pi_{r}(a|g,s)
with
its definition when
\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h-1}=g\wedge s_{h-1}=s)>0
as in
the opposite case the entire formula takes on value zero.
∎

C.1.2
\Pi(r^{E})
is too Large for some
r^{E}

Consider any reward
r^{E}:\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\times\llbracket H\rrbracket\to[0,1]
that assigns, to every possible
trajectory
\omega\in\Omega
, a different return value

G(\omega;r^{E})\in[0,H-1]
. Observe that rewards
r^{E}
of this kind exist as the
number of possible trajectories
|\Omega|=\mathcal{O}((SA)^{H-1})
is finite, while the
set of possible return values that we can assign to each
s,a,h
is infinite
(continuous)
[0,1]
.
An example of a reward of this kind was provided in the proof of Theorem

3.1
:

\displaystyle r^{E}_{h}(s,a)=10^{-hSA-x_{s}(s)A-x_{a}(a)}\qquad\forall(s,a,h)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\times\llbracket H\rrbracket,

where
x_{s}:\mathcal{S}\to\mathbb{N},x_{a}:\mathcal{A}\to\mathbb{N}
are arbitrary injective functions mapping
the set of states and actions to the set of natural numbers. Intuitively, given
the return
G(\omega;r^{E})
of any trajectory:

\displaystyle\omega=(s_{1},a_{1},s_{2},a_{2},\dotsc,s_{h-1},a_{h-1}),

we can easily reconstruct which
s,a,h
actually belong to
\omega
by checking
which decimal numbers in
G(\omega;r^{E})
are “flagged”.

Given a reward
r^{E}
of this kind, we have that
|\mathcal{G}_{r^{E}}|
is in the same
order as
|\Omega|=\mathcal{O}((SA)^{H-1})
, and so the set of policies
\Pi(r^{E})

defined in Section
4.1
reduces to the whole set of
non-Markovian policies
\Pi^{\text{NM}}
, with all its disadvantages.

C.2
No Interaction Setting

In Appendix
C.2.1
, we prove Theorem
4.3
, while
in Appendix
C.2.2
we provide
additional discussion on Theorem
4.3
.

C.2.1
Proof of Theorem
4.3

See
4.3

Proof.

We can write:

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{E}},\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r^{E}}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(1)}}}{\leq}\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{E}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\pi_{r^{E}_{\theta}}}_{r^{E}}}\Big)+\mathcal{W}\Big({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\pi_{r^{E}_{\theta}}}_{r^{E}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\pi_{r^{E}_{\theta}}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}}}\Big)+\mathcal{W}\Big({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\pi_{r^{E}_{\theta}}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}}}\Big)+\mathcal{W}\Big({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}}},\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r^{E}}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(2)}}}{\leq}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}2H\theta}+\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta^{\pi_{r^{E}_{\theta}}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}},\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(3)}}}{\leq}2H\theta+{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}H\Big\|}\eta^{\pi_{r^{E}_{\theta}}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}}-\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Big\|_{1}}

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(4)}}}{\leq}2H\theta+H{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sum\limits_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{E}_{\theta},h}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{r^{E}_{\theta}}}(G_{h}=g\wedge s_{h}=s)\Big\|\pi_{r^{E}_{\theta},h}(\cdot|s,g)-\widehat{\pi}_{h}(\cdot|s,g)\Big\|_{1}}

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(5)}}}{=}2H\theta+H\sum\limits_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{E}_{\theta},h}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}}(G_{h}=g\wedge s_{h}=s)\Big\|\pi_{r^{E}_{\theta},h}(\cdot|s,g)-\widehat{\pi}_{h}(\cdot|s,g)\Big\|_{1}

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(6)}}}{\leq}2H\theta+H\epsilon^{\prime},

where at (1) we apply triangle’s inequality, at (2) we apply Lemma
4.2
and Lemma
C.1
twice, at (3) we use
Particular Case 6.13 of
Villani 2008
, which tells us that we can
upper bound the Wasserstein distance between two distributions supported on set

\mathcal{X}
by the diameter of
\mathcal{X}
(
\max_{x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}}|x-x^{\prime}|
) times the one norm
between the two distributions. Since
\mathcal{X}=[0,H]
in our case, we get the
expression written above.
At (4) we apply Lemma
C.3
with the notation defined
in that lemma, observing that policies
\pi_{r^{E}_{\theta}}
and
\widehat{\pi}

satisfy the hypothesis for reward
r^{E}_{\theta}
.
At (5) we use Lemma
C.2
observing that the random return

G_{h}
is in terms of reward
r^{E}_{\theta}
, and recalling the definition of

\pi_{r^{E}_{\theta}}
.
Lastly, at (6) we apply Lemma
C.4
with accuracy
\epsilon^{\prime}
.

The result follows by imposing that
\epsilon^{\prime}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2H}
and

2H\theta\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}
, which can be achieved by taking

\epsilon^{\prime}=\frac{\epsilon}{2H}
and
\theta=\frac{\epsilon}{4H}
, and by
observing that:

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{G}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}|\mathcal{G}_{r^{E}_{\theta},h}|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}|\mathcal{Y}^{\theta}_{h}|

\displaystyle=\sum_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}|\{0,\theta,2\theta,\dotsc,\lfloor(h-1)/\theta\rfloor\theta\}|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}(1+(h-1)/\theta)

\displaystyle=H(1-1/\theta)+\frac{1}{\theta}\sum_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}h

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(10)}}}{=}H(1-1/\theta)+\frac{H(H+1)}{2\theta}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{H^{2}}{\theta}

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(11)}}}{\leq}\frac{4H^{3}}{\epsilon},

where at (10) we used the formula for arithmetic sums, and at (11) we used the
previous choice
\theta=\frac{\epsilon}{4H}
.

Replacing into the number of samples in Lemma
C.4
(and
also
\epsilon^{\prime}=\frac{\epsilon}{2H}
) we get the result:

\displaystyle N\geq\frac{3072SH^{6}\ln\frac{8SH^{3}}{\delta\epsilon}}{\epsilon^{3}}\bigg(\ln\frac{8SH^{3}}{\delta\epsilon}+(A-1)\ln\Big(\frac{2048eSH^{6}\ln\frac{8SH^{3}}{\delta\epsilon}}{\epsilon^{3}}\Big)\bigg).

By using
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}
notation to hide logarithmic terms in

S,A,H,\frac{1}{\epsilon},\ln\frac{1}{\delta}
, we get the result.
∎

Lemma C.3

(Error Propagation)
.

Let
\mathcal{M}_{r}
be any MDP.
For any pair of policies
\pi,\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi^{\text{NM}}
such that, for all

h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket
,
a\in\mathcal{A}
,
s\in\mathcal{S}
and
\omega,\omega^{\prime}\in\Omega_{h}
with

G(\omega;r)=G(\omega^{\prime};r)
:

\displaystyle\pi(a|\omega,s)=\pi(a|\omega^{\prime},s)\qquad\wedge\qquad\pi^{\prime}(a|\omega,s)=\pi^{\prime}(a|\omega^{\prime},s),

it holds that:

\displaystyle\Big\|\eta^{\pi}_{r}-\eta^{\pi^{\prime}}_{r}\Big\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h}=g\wedge s_{h}=s)\Big\|\pi_{h}(\cdot|s,g)-\pi_{h}^{\prime}(\cdot|s,g)\Big\|_{1},

where
\mathcal{G}_{r,h}\coloneqq\{g\in[0,h-1]\,|\,\exists\omega\in\Omega_{h}:\,G(\omega;r)=g\}
for all
h\in\llbracket H+1\rrbracket
,

G_{h}\coloneqq\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{h-1}r_{h^{\prime}}(s_{h^{\prime}},a_{h^{\prime}})
denotes the
random

return at stage
h
, and
\pi_{h}(\cdot|s,g)
and
\pi_{h}^{\prime}(\cdot|s,g)
denote
the unique probability with which the policies
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
prescribe
actions in
s
at
h
under any trajectory
\omega\in\Omega_{h}
with

G(\omega;r)=g
.

Proof.

To prove the result, we first demonstrate by induction that, for
all
h\in\llbracket 2,H\rrbracket
, it holds that:

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h}\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\Big|\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h}=g\wedge s_{h}=s)-\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(G_{h}=g\wedge s_{h}=s)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\sum\limits_{h^{\prime}\in\llbracket h-1\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h^{\prime}}=g\wedge s_{h^{\prime}}=s)\Big\|\pi_{h^{\prime}}(\cdot|s,g)-\pi_{h^{\prime}}^{\prime}(\cdot|s,g)\Big\|_{1}.

We remark that, by hypothesis, both
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
prescribe the same actions
when faced with trajectories
\omega,\omega^{\prime}
with the same return

G(\omega;r)=G(\omega^{\prime};r)
that are followed by the same state
s
.

We begin with the base case
h=2
. We can write:

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,2}\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\Big|\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{2}=g\wedge s_{2}=s^{\prime})-\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(G_{2}=g\wedge s_{2}=s^{\prime})\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,2}\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\Big|\mathbb{P}^{\pi}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}r_{1}(s_{1},a_{1})}=g\wedge s_{2}=s^{\prime})-\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}r_{1}(s_{1},a_{1})}=g\wedge s_{2}=s^{\prime})\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,2}\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\Big|\mathop{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sum}}\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}r_{1}(s,a)=g}\end{subarray}}\Big({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(s_{2}=s^{\prime}|s,a)\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(a_{1}=a|s)\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(s_{1}=s)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(s_{2}=s^{\prime}|s,a)\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(a_{1}=a|s)\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(s_{1}=s)}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(1)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,2}\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\Big|\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{1}(s,a)=g\end{subarray}}\Big({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p(s^{\prime}|s,a)\mathds{1}\{s=s_{0}\}\pi(a|s)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p(s^{\prime}|s,a)\mathds{1}\{s=s_{0}\}\pi^{\prime}(a|s)}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(2)}}}{\leq}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,2}\end{subarray}}\mathop{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sum}}\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}a\in\mathcal{A}:}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}r_{1}(s_{0},a)=g}\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}p(s^{\prime}|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s_{0}},a){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Big|}\pi(a|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s_{0}})-\pi^{\prime}(a|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s_{0}}){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Big|}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,2}\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{1}(s_{0},a)=g\end{subarray}}\Big|\pi(a|s_{0})-\pi^{\prime}(a|s_{0})\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sum\limits_{a\in\mathcal{A}}}\Big|\pi(a|s_{0})-\pi^{\prime}(a|s_{0})\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\Big\|\pi(\cdot|s_{0})-\pi^{\prime}(\cdot|s_{0})\Big\|_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(3)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{h^{\prime}\in\llbracket 1\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h^{\prime}}=g\wedge s_{h^{\prime}}=s)\Big\|\pi_{h^{\prime}}(\cdot|s,g)-\pi_{h^{\prime}}^{\prime}(\cdot|s,g)\Big\|_{1},

where at (1) we realize that in
\mathcal{M}_{r}
the initial state is always
s_{0}
, and
that the transition model is Markovian and independent of the policy, at (2) we
apply triangle’s inequality and keep only
s_{0}
because of the indicator, and at
(3) we have simply rewritten the expression in a more convenient way for proving
the result (note that
\llbracket 1\rrbracket=\{1\}
and
\mathcal{G}_{r^{\prime},1}=\{0\}
and

\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h^{\prime}}=g\wedge s_{1}=s)=\mathds{1}\{g=0\wedge s=s_{0}\}
).

Now, let us consider any stage
h\in\llbracket 3,H\rrbracket
. Let us make the inductive
hypothesis that:

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h-1}\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\Big|\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h-1}=g\wedge s_{h-1}=s)-\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(G_{h-1}=g\wedge s_{h-1}=s)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\sum\limits_{h^{\prime}\in\llbracket h-2\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h^{\prime}}=g\wedge s_{h^{\prime}}=s)\cdot\Big\|\pi_{h^{\prime}}(\cdot|s,g)-\pi_{h^{\prime}}^{\prime}(\cdot|s,g)\Big\|_{1}.

Then, we can write (we use symbol
\omega_{h}
to denote the random trajectory

(s_{1},a_{1},\dotsc,s_{h},a_{h})
up to stage
h
):

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h}\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\Big|\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h}=g^{\prime}\wedge s_{h}=s^{\prime})-\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(G_{h}=g^{\prime}\wedge s_{h}=s^{\prime})\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sum\limits_{g^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h}}\sum\limits_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\Big|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h-1}}}\Big(

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\mathbb{P}^{\pi}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}G_{h-1}=g\wedge r_{h-1}(s_{h-1},a_{h-1})=g^{\prime}-g}\wedge s_{h}=s^{\prime})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}G_{h-1}=g\wedge r_{h-1}(s_{h-1},a_{h-1})=g^{\prime}-g}\wedge s_{h}=s^{\prime})\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sum\limits_{g^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h}}\sum\limits_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\Big|\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h-1}}\mathop{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sum}}\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1}:}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}G(\omega;r)=g}\end{subarray}}\mathop{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sum}}\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}r_{h-1}(s,a)=g^{\prime}-g}\end{subarray}}\Big(

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\mathbb{P}^{\pi}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s\wedge a_{h-1}=a}\wedge s_{h}=s^{\prime})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s\wedge a_{h-1}=a}\wedge s_{h}=s^{\prime})\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(4)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{g^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h}}\sum\limits_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\Big|\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1}:\\
G(\omega;r)=g\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}(s,a)=g^{\prime}-g\end{subarray}}\Big(

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\mathbb{P}^{\pi}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s})\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(a_{h-1}=a\wedge s_{h}=s^{\prime}|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\omega,s})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s})\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(a_{h-1}=a\wedge s_{h}=s^{\prime}|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\omega,s})\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(5)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{g^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h}}\sum\limits_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\Big|\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1}:\\
G(\omega;r)=g\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}(s,a)=g^{\prime}-g\end{subarray}}\Big(

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pi(a|\omega,s)p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pi^{\prime}(a|\omega,s)p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sum\limits_{g^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h}}\sum\limits_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\Big|\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1}:\\
G(\omega;r)=g\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}(s,a)=g^{\prime}-g\end{subarray}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)}\Big(

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)\pi(a|\omega,s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)\pi^{\prime}(a|\omega,s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)\pi^{\prime}(a|\omega,s)}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(6)}}}{\leq}\sum\limits_{g^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h}}\sum\limits_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1}:\\
G(\omega;r)=g\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}(s,a)=g^{\prime}-g\end{subarray}}p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Big|\pi(a|\omega,s)-\pi^{\prime}(a|\omega,s)\Big|}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sum\limits_{g^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h}}\sum\limits_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h-1}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Big|}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1}:\\
G(\omega;r)=g\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}(s,a)=g^{\prime}-g\end{subarray}}p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)\pi^{\prime}(a|\omega,s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)-\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)}\Big){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Big|}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(7)}}}{\leq}\sum\limits_{g^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h}}\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1}:\\
G(\omega;r)=g\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}(s,a)=g^{\prime}-g\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)\Big|\pi(a|\omega,s)-\pi^{\prime}(a|\omega,s)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sum\limits_{g^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h}}\sum\limits_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h-1}}\mathop{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sum}}\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}r_{h-1}(s,a)=g^{\prime}-g}\end{subarray}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Big|}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1}:\\
G(\omega;r)=g\end{subarray}}\pi^{\prime}(a|\omega,s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big(\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)-\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)\Big){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Big|}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(8)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h-1}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\end{subarray}}\Big|\pi_{h-1}(a|s,g)-\pi_{h-1}^{\prime}(a|s,g)\Big|}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1}:\\
G(\omega;r)=g\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sum\limits_{g^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h}}\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}(s,a)=g^{\prime}-g\end{subarray}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pi_{h-1}^{\prime}(a|s,g)}\Big|\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1}:\\
G(\omega;r)=g\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big(\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)-\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\end{subarray}}\Big|\pi_{h-1}(a|s,g)-\pi_{h-1}^{\prime}(a|s,g)\Big|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h-1}=g\wedge s_{h-1}=s)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sum\limits_{g^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h}}\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}(s,a)=g^{\prime}-g\end{subarray}}\pi_{h-1}^{\prime}(a|s,g)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\Big|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h-1}=g\wedge s_{h-1}=s)}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(G_{h-1}=g\wedge s_{h-1}=s)}\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(9)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h-1}}\sum\limits_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s\in\mathcal{S}}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h-1}=g\wedge s_{h-1}=s){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Big\|\pi_{h-1}(\cdot|s,g)-\pi_{h-1}^{\prime}(\cdot|s,g)\Big\|_{1}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h-1}}\sum\limits_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s\in\mathcal{S}}}\Big|\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h-1}=g\wedge s_{h-1}=s)-\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(G_{h-1}=g\wedge s_{h-1}=s)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(10)}}}{\leq}\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h-1}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h-1}=g\wedge s_{h-1}=s)\Big\|\pi_{h-1}(\cdot|s,g)-\pi_{h-1}^{\prime}(\cdot|s,g)\Big\|_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sum\limits_{h^{\prime}\in\llbracket h-2\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h^{\prime}}=g\wedge s_{h^{\prime}}=s)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\cdot\Big\|\pi_{h^{\prime}}(\cdot|s,g)-\pi_{h^{\prime}}^{\prime}(\cdot|s,g)\Big\|_{1}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sum\limits_{h^{\prime}\in\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}h-1}\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h^{\prime}}=g\wedge s_{h^{\prime}}=s)\cdot\Big\|\pi_{h^{\prime}}(\cdot|s,g)-\pi_{h^{\prime}}^{\prime}(\cdot|s,g)\Big\|_{1},

where at (4) we use the chain rule of conditional probabilities, at (5) we do it
again, and we recognize the policies
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
, and also that the
transition model is Markovian, at (6) we use triangle’s inequality to split the
summations and bring the absolute value inside, at (7), in the first term, we
note that
p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)
is the only term that depends on
s^{\prime}
and that it
sums to 1, while in the second term we exchange the order of two summations and
apply triangle’s inequality to bring one inside, at (8), in the first term, we
first remove the summation on
g^{\prime}
along with the indicator function that
forces us to consider a subset of state-action pairs, and then we exchange two
other summations and note that the policies do not depend by hypothesis on the
entire past trajectory, but just on the return so far. Instead, in the second
term, we use that
\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)=1
, and also that, by
hypothesis,
\pi^{\prime}
does not depend on the entire past trajectory, but just on

g
. At (9), i.a., we use that
\sum_{g^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h}}\mathds{1}\{r_{h-1}(s,a)=g^{\prime}-g\}=1
and that

\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\pi_{h-1}^{\prime}(a|s,g)=1
. Finally, at (10), we apply the inductive
hypothesis.

Thanks to this result, we can finally prove the claim in the lemma, using
passages analogous to those above, with the difference that we do not have the
summation over the states at the current stage (i.e.,
H+1
):

\displaystyle\Big\|\eta^{\pi}_{r}-\eta^{\pi^{\prime}}_{r}\Big\|_{1}=\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,H+1}}\Big|\eta^{\pi}_{r}(g)-\eta^{\pi^{\prime}}_{r}(g)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,H+1}}\Big|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{H+1}=g)}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(G_{H+1}=g)}\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,H+1}}\Big|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sum\limits_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\sum\limits_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}\sum\limits_{g^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{r,H}}\sum\limits_{\omega\in\Omega_{H}}\mathds{1}\{r_{H}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})=g-g^{\prime},G(\omega;r)=g^{\prime}\}}\Big(

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\mathbb{P}^{\pi}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\omega_{H-1}=\omega\wedge s_{H}=s^{\prime}\wedge a_{H}=a^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\omega_{H-1}=\omega\wedge s_{H}=s^{\prime}\wedge a_{H}=a^{\prime}}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,H+1}}\Big|\sum\limits_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\sum\limits_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}\sum\limits_{g^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{r,H}}\sum\limits_{\omega\in\Omega_{H}}\mathds{1}\{r_{H}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})=g-g^{\prime},G(\omega;r)=g^{\prime}\}\Big(

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(\omega_{H-1}=\omega\wedge s_{H}=s^{\prime}){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pi(a^{\prime}|\omega,s^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(\omega_{H-1}=\omega\wedge s_{H}=s^{\prime}){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pi^{\prime}(a^{\prime}|\omega,s^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(\omega_{H-1}=\omega\wedge s_{H}=s^{\prime})\pi^{\prime}(a^{\prime}|\omega,s^{\prime})}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(11)}}}{\leq}\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,H}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{H}=g\wedge s_{H}=s)\Big\|\pi_{H}(\cdot|s,g)-\pi_{H}^{\prime}(\cdot|s,g)\Big\|_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,H}}\sum\limits_{{s\in\mathcal{S}}}\Big|\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{H}=g\wedge s_{H}=s)-\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(G_{H}=g\wedge s_{H}=s)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(12)}}}{\leq}\sum\limits_{h^{\prime}\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{G}_{r,h^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h^{\prime}}=g\wedge s_{h^{\prime}}=s)\Big\|\pi_{h^{\prime}}(\cdot|s,g)-\pi_{h^{\prime}}^{\prime}(\cdot|s,g)\Big\|_{1},

where at (11) we made the same passages as above, all in one, and at (12) we
applied the result proved earlier by induction.

This concludes the proof. Note that, from a high-level perspective, our proof
approach resembles the “reduction to supervised learning” approach made in

Rajaraman et al. 2020
for stochastic policies, with
the difference that we work in an augmented state-space MDP.
∎

Lemma C.4

(Concentration)
.

Let
\epsilon\in(0,H]
and
\delta\in(0,1)
.
Let
\mathcal{M}_{r^{E}}
be any MDP,
\pi^{E}\in\Pi^{\text{NM}}
be any expert’s policy,
and
\widehat{\pi}
be the output of Algorithm
1
.
Then, with probability
1-\delta
, we have that (we use the notation in Lemma

C.3
):

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{E}_{\theta},h}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(G_{h}=g\wedge s_{h}=s)\Big\|\pi_{r^{E}_{\theta},h}(\cdot|s,g)-\widehat{\pi}_{h}(\cdot|s,g)\Big\|_{1}\leq\epsilon,

with a number of samples:

\displaystyle N\leq\frac{193SH\overline{\mathcal{G}}\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}}{\epsilon^{2}}\bigg(\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}+(A-1)\ln\Big(\frac{128eSH\overline{\mathcal{G}}\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}}{\epsilon^{2}}\Big)\bigg),

where
\overline{\mathcal{G}}\coloneqq\sum_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}|\mathcal{G}_{r^{E}_{\theta},h}|
.

Proof.

We can write:

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{E}_{\theta},h}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(G_{h}=g\wedge s_{h}=s)\Big\|\pi_{r^{E}_{\theta},h}(\cdot|s,g)-\widehat{\pi}_{h}(\cdot|s,g)\Big\|_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sum\limits_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{E}_{\theta},h}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(G_{h}=g\wedge s_{h}=s)\sum\limits_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\Big|\pi_{r^{E}_{\theta},h}(a|g,s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\Big({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\frac{M_{h}(s,g,a)}{\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g,a^{\prime})}\mathds{1}\Big\{\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g,a^{\prime})>0\Big\}+\frac{1}{A}\mathds{1}\Big\{\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g,a^{\prime})=0\Big\}}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(1)}}}{\leq}\sum\limits_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{E}_{\theta},h}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(G_{h}=g\wedge s_{h}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}2\sqrt{2}\sqrt{\frac{\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}+(A-1)\ln\big(e\big(1+\frac{\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g,a^{\prime})}{A-1}\big)\big)}{\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g,a^{\prime})}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(2)}}}{\leq}2\sqrt{2}\sqrt{\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}+(A-1)\ln\Big(e\Big(1+\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}N}}{A-1}\Big)\Big)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\sum\limits_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{E}_{\theta},h}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(G_{h}=g\wedge s_{h}=s)\sqrt{\frac{1}{\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g,a^{\prime})}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(3)}}}{\leq}2\sqrt{2}\sqrt{\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}+(A-1)\ln\Big(e\Big(1+\frac{N}{A-1}\Big)\Big)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\sum\limits_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{E}_{\theta},h}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\sqrt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\frac{8\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(G_{h}=g\wedge s_{h}=s)}{N}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}8\sqrt{\frac{\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}}{N}}}\sqrt{\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}+(A-1)\ln\Big(e\Big(1+\frac{N}{A-1}\Big)\Big)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\sum\limits_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{E}_{\theta},h}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\sqrt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(G_{h}=g\wedge s_{h}=s)}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(4)}}}{\leq}8\sqrt{\frac{\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}}{N}}\sqrt{\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}+(A-1)\ln\Big(e\Big(1+\frac{N}{A-1}\Big)\Big)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\sum\limits_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sqrt{S|\mathcal{G}_{r^{E}_{\theta},h}|}}\sqrt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{E}_{\theta},h}\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(G_{h}=g\wedge s_{h}=s)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=8\sqrt{\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}S}\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}}{N}}\sqrt{\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}+(A-1)\ln\Big(e\Big(1+\frac{N}{A-1}\Big)\Big)}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sum\limits_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}\sqrt{|\mathcal{G}_{r^{E}_{\theta},h}|}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(5)}}}{\leq}8\sqrt{\frac{S{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}H\overline{\mathcal{G}}}\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}}{N}}\sqrt{\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}+(A-1)\ln\Big(e\Big(1+\frac{N}{A-1}\Big)\Big)},

where at (1) we use that, if
\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g,a^{\prime})=0
, then:

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\Big|\pi_{r^{E}_{\theta},h}(a|g,s)-\Big(\frac{M_{h}(s,g,a)}{\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g,a^{\prime})}\mathds{1}\{\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g,a^{\prime})>0\}+\frac{1}{A}\mathds{1}\{\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g,a^{\prime})=0\}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sum\limits_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\Big|\pi_{r^{E}_{\theta},h}(a|g,s)-\frac{1}{A}\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq 2,

as we the total variation distance between two probability distributions
cannot exceed 1. Instead, if
\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g,a^{\prime})>0
,
conditioning

on
\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g,a^{\prime})
, at all
s,g
where
\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(G_{h}=g\wedge s_{h}=s)>0
, we note that
M_{h}(s,g,a)/\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g,a^{\prime})
is the
empirical vector of probabilities of
\pi_{r^{E}_{\theta},h}(a|g,s)
(recall its
definition from Eq.
6
), thus we can
apply Lemma 8 of
Kaufmann et al. 2021
to get that, for any

\delta\in(0,1)
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}\Big(KL\Big(\frac{M_{h}(s,g,\cdot)}{\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g,a^{\prime})}\Big\|\pi_{r^{E}_{\theta},h}(\cdot|g,s)\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\frac{\ln\frac{1}{\delta}+(A-1)\ln\big(e\big(1+\frac{\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g,a^{\prime})}{A-1}\big)\big)}{\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g,a^{\prime})}\Big)\geq 1-\delta.

Combining this result with the Pinsker’s inequality, that tells us that

\|x-y\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{2KL(x\|y)}
, and with a union bound over all

h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket
,
s\in\mathcal{S}
,
g\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{E}_{\theta},h}
, we get the passage in (1)
w.p.
1-\delta/2
. Note that we add an additional 2 for the case

\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g,a^{\prime})=0
, and we define
\overline{\mathcal{G}}\coloneqq\sum_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}|\mathcal{G}_{r^{E}_{\theta},h}|
.
At (2) we bound
\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g,a^{\prime})\leq N
, and bring that quantity
outside, at (3) we apply Lemma A.1 of
Xie et al. 2021
, after having
noticed that
\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g,a^{\prime})\sim\text{Bin}\Big(N,\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(G_{h}=g\wedge s_{h}=s)\Big)
, and make it hold for
all
s,g,h
w.p.
1-\delta/2
.
At (4) and (5) we apply the Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality.

Now, we impose that this quantity is smaller than
\epsilon
:

\displaystyle 8\sqrt{\frac{SH\overline{\mathcal{G}}\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}}{N}}\sqrt{\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}+(A-1)\ln\Big(e\Big(1+\frac{N}{A-1}\Big)\Big)}\leq\epsilon

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\iff N\geq\frac{64SH\overline{\mathcal{G}}\ln^{2}\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}}{\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{64SH\overline{\mathcal{G}}(A-1)\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}}{\epsilon^{2}}\ln\Big(\frac{eN}{A-1}+e\Big).

Thanks to Lemma J.3 of
Lazzati et al. 2024
, we know that this inequality
is satisfied with:

\displaystyle N\leq\frac{128SH\overline{\mathcal{G}}\ln^{2}\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}}{\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{192SH\overline{\mathcal{G}}(A-1)\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}}{\epsilon^{2}}\ln\Big(\frac{128eSH\overline{\mathcal{G}}\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}}{\epsilon^{2}}\Big)+A-1.

Rearranging and applying a final union bound concludes the proof.
∎

C.2.2
Additional Discussion on RS-BC

First, we observe that the sample complexity bound in Theorem
4.3

cannot be improved if we extend our analysis with that of

Foster et al. 2024
. Indeed,
Foster et al. 2024
also
provides an
1/\epsilon^{2}
dependence as our proof, that combined with the
additional
1/\epsilon
due to discretization, would give the same

1/\epsilon^{3}
rate. Moreover,
Foster et al. 2024
would not allow to
improve even the
H^{6}
dependence in the horizon in our proof, as their
Corollary 3.1 combined with a simple variation of our proof that considers an
MDP with an augmented state space, would still provide an
H^{4}
dependence that
should be combined with the
H^{2}
arising from bounding the Wasserstein with
H

times the total variation, and taking the square.
Note that these considerations on
Foster et al. 2024
implicitly
assumed that the proof of
Foster et al. 2024
can be extended to
non-Markovian expert’s policies with the same rate, which has to be demonstrated
as well.

Second, we mention that
Rajaraman et al. 2020

provides for IL a
1/\epsilon
dependence instead of
1/\epsilon^{2}
. However,
note we remark that the result is in
expectation
, and not with
high
probability
, as remarked also by
Foster et al. 2024
in their
footnote 21. Indeed, this explains why the
1/\epsilon
rate of

Rajaraman et al. 2020
seems to overcome the

1/\epsilon^{2}
in the lower bound of Theorem G.1 of

Foster et al. 2024
.

Lastly, we mention that
RS
-
BC
(and also the theoretical guarantees in Theorem

4.3
) can be easily extended to the setting in which
r^{E}
is unknown
but
observed
, namely, in which expert’s trajectories are
state-action-reward trajectories
(s_{1},a_{1},r_{1},\dotsc)
. Indeed, looking at
Algorithm
1
, note that the computation of the returns

G(\omega;r^{E})
does not require knowledge of
r^{E}
in state-action pairs never
observed. This is different from
RS
-
KT
, in which we require knowledge of
r^{E}
everywhere.

C.3
Known-Transition Setting

In Appendix
C.3.1
, we prove Theorem
4.4
, while
in Appendix
C.3.2
, we write down explicitly the LP in Eq.
(
10
).

C.3.1
Proof of Theorem
4.4

See
4.4

Proof.

We begin by showing that the estimate of return distribution
\widehat{\eta}

computed by
RS
-
KT
at Line
2
is close to
the expert’s return distribution with high probability.
To this aim, we write:

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{E}},\widehat{\eta}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(1)}}}{\leq}\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{E}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}}}\Big)+\mathcal{W}\Big({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}}},\widehat{\eta}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(2)}}}{\leq}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}H\theta/2}+\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}},\widehat{\eta}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(3)}}}{=}H\theta/2+{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\int\limits_{0}^{H}\Big|F_{\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}}}(x)-F_{\widehat{\eta}}(x)\Big|dx}

\displaystyle\leq H\theta/2+\int\limits_{0}^{H}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sup\limits_{x^{\prime}\in[0,H]}}\Big|F_{\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(x^{\prime})}-F_{\widehat{\eta}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(x^{\prime})}\Big|dx

\displaystyle=H\theta/2+\sup\limits_{x^{\prime}\in[0,H]}\Big|F_{\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}}}(x^{\prime})-F_{\widehat{\eta}}(x^{\prime})\Big|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\int\limits_{0}^{H}dx}

\displaystyle=H\theta/2+{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}H}\sup\limits_{x^{\prime}\in[0,H]}\Big|F_{\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}}}(x^{\prime})-F_{\widehat{\eta}}(x^{\prime})\Big|

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(4)}}}{\leq}H\theta/2+H{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\epsilon^{\prime}},

where at (1) we use triangle’s inequality, at (2) we apply Lemma
C.1
, at (3) we use symbol
F_{q}
for the distribution function of
any probability measure
q
, at (4) we apply the DKW inequality

(
Kiefer & Wolfowitz 1959
;
Massart 1990
)
, as
F_{\widehat{\eta}}(x)=\sum_{x^{\prime}\leq x}\widehat{\eta}(x^{\prime})=\sum_{x^{\prime}\leq x}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in\llbracket N\rrbracket}\mathds{1}\{\sum_{h=1}^{H}r^{E}_{\theta,h}(s_{h}^{i},a_{h}^{i})=x^{\prime}\}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in\llbracket N\rrbracket}\mathds{1}\{\sum_{h=1}^{H}r^{E}_{\theta,h}(s_{h}^{i},a_{h}^{i})\leq x^{\prime}\}
corresponds to the empirical
distribution function of
\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}}
. Specifically, in our
setting, the DKW inequality tells us that, for any
\epsilon^{\prime}>0
, it holds that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}\Big(\sup\limits_{x^{\prime}\in[0,H]}\Big|F_{\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}}}(x^{\prime})-F_{\widehat{\eta}}(x^{\prime})\Big|\leq\epsilon^{\prime}\Big)\geq 1-2e^{-2N(\epsilon^{\prime})^{2}}.

(13)

By imposing the term on the right hand side to be
1-\delta
, and solving w.r.t.

N
, we get that:

\displaystyle N\leq\frac{1}{2(\epsilon^{\prime})^{2}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta}.

Now, building on this result, we can write:

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{E}},\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r^{E}}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(5)}}}{\leq}\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{E}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\widehat{\eta}}\Big)+\mathcal{W}\Big({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\widehat{\eta}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}}}\Big)+\mathcal{W}\Big({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}}},\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r^{E}}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(6)}}}{\leq}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}H\theta/2+H\epsilon^{\prime}}+\mathcal{W}\Big(\widehat{\eta},\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}}\Big)+\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}},\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r^{E}}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(7)}}}{\leq}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}H\theta}+H\epsilon^{\prime}+\mathcal{W}\Big(\widehat{\eta},\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(8)}}}{=}H\theta+H\epsilon^{\prime}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\min\limits_{\pi\in\Pi(r^{E}_{\theta})}}\mathcal{W}\Big(\widehat{\eta},\eta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pi}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(9)}}}{\leq}H\theta+H\epsilon^{\prime}+\mathcal{W}\Big(\widehat{\eta},\eta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pi_{r^{E}_{\theta}}}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(10)}}}{\leq}H\theta+H\epsilon^{\prime}+\mathcal{W}\Big(\widehat{\eta},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{E}}}\Big)+\mathcal{W}\Big({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{E}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\pi_{r^{E}_{\theta}}}_{r^{E}}}\Big)+\mathcal{W}\Big({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\pi_{r^{E}_{\theta}}}_{r^{E}}},\eta^{\pi_{r^{E}_{\theta}}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(11)}}}{\leq}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}2H\theta}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}2H\epsilon^{\prime}}+\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{E}},\eta^{\pi_{r^{E}_{\theta}}}_{r^{E}}\Big)+\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta^{\pi_{r^{E}_{\theta}}}_{r^{E}},\eta^{\pi_{r^{E}_{\theta}}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(12)}}}{\leq}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\frac{5}{2}H\theta}+2H\epsilon^{\prime}+\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{E}},\eta^{\pi_{r^{E}_{\theta}}}_{r^{E}}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(12)}}}{\leq}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\frac{7}{2}H\theta}+2H\epsilon^{\prime},

where at (5) we apply triangle’s inequality, at (6) we use the result above,
at (7) we apply Lemma
C.1
, at (8) we use the
definition of
\widehat{\pi}
and the hypothesis of solving the minimization
problem exactly, at (9) we upper bound with a specific choice of policy, i.e.,

\pi_{r^{E}_{\theta}}
(recall Eq.
6
), at
(10) we apply triangle’s inequality again, at (11) we apply the result above
again, at (12) we use again Lemma
C.1
, and
finally, at (13), we apply Lemma
4.2
.

If we now choose
\theta=\epsilon/(7H)
, and
\epsilon^{\prime}=\epsilon/(4H)
, we get that,
with probability
1-\delta
, it holds that:

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{E}},\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r^{E}}\Big)\leq\epsilon,

with:

\displaystyle N\leq\frac{2H^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta}.

∎

C.3.2
Explicit Formulation of the LP

The optimization problem in Eq. (
10
) can be written more
explicitly as follows:

\displaystyle\min\limits_{d\in\mathbb{R}^{SAH|\mathcal{Y}^{\theta}|}_{\geq 0},\eta\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{Y}^{\theta}|}_{\geq 0},t\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{Y}^{\theta}|}_{\geq 0},x\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{Y}^{\theta}|}_{\geq 0}}\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{Y}^{\theta}}t(g)

\displaystyle\qquad\text{s.t. }\sum\limits_{(s,a,g)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{Y}^{\theta}}d_{1}(s,g,a)=1

(14)

\displaystyle\qquad\sum\limits_{a\in\mathcal{A}}d_{1}(s_{0},0,a)=1

(15)

\displaystyle\qquad\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}d_{h}(s,g,a)=\sum_{s^{\prime},g^{\prime},a^{\prime}}d_{h-1}(s^{\prime},g^{\prime},a^{\prime})p_{h-1}(s|s^{\prime},a^{\prime})\mathds{1}\{r^{E}_{\theta,h}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})=g-g^{\prime}\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\forall(s,g,h)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{Y}^{\theta}\times\{2,\dotsc,H\}

(16)

\displaystyle\qquad\eta(g)=\sum\limits_{(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}}d_{H}(s,g-r_{\theta,H}^{E}(s,a),a)\qquad\forall g\in\mathcal{Y}^{\theta}

(17)

\displaystyle\qquad x(g)=\sum\limits_{g^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Y}^{\theta}:\;g^{\prime}\leq g}\Big(\eta(g^{\prime})-\widehat{\eta}(g^{\prime})\Big)\qquad\forall g\in\mathcal{Y}^{\theta}

(18)

\displaystyle\qquad-t(g)\leq x(g)\leq t(g)\qquad\forall g\in\mathcal{Y}^{\theta}

(19)

where Eqs. (
14
)-(
15
)-(
16
) denote
the flow constraints, i.e., define the set of feasible occupancy measures
\mathcal{K}
,
Eq. (
17
) enforces that
\eta
is the return distribution in

\overline{\mathcal{M}}
corresponding to occupancy measure
d
, while Eqs.
(
18
)-(
19
) permit to rewrite the Wasserstein
distance in a linear manner.

Observe that the number of optimization variables is

SAH|\mathcal{Y}^{\theta}|+|\mathcal{Y}^{\theta}|=\mathcal{O}(SAH|\mathcal{Y}^{\theta}|)
, while the number of
constraints is
2+(H-1)S|\mathcal{Y}^{\theta}|+3|\mathcal{Y}^{\theta}|=\mathcal{O}(SAH|\mathcal{Y}^{\theta}|)
.

C.4
When
r^{E}
belongs to a finite set of
d
Rewards

In this appendix, we consider a variant of the known-reward setting, in which

r^{E}
is unknown, but we have knowledge of a set
\mathcal{R}=\{r^{1},\dotsc,r^{d}\}

containing
d\geq 1
reward functions, and we also know that
r^{E}\in\mathcal{R}
.
We consider the following robust variant of RDM for this setting:

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}\in\argmin_{\pi\in\Pi^{\text{NM}}}\max\limits_{r\in\mathcal{R}}\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta_{r}^{\pi},\eta_{r}^{\pi^{E}}\Big).

(20)

To tackle this problem, we will proceed to Section
4
, by
considering the
cartesian product
of all the rewards in set
\mathcal{R}
.
Specifically, we first present a class of non-Markovian policies sufficiently
expressive for addressing this task, and then we present two variants of
RS
-
BC
and
RS
-
KT
. Crucially, we will have exponential dependencies in the number of
rewards
d
for both the computational and sample complexities.

Let us begin by presenting
\Pi(\mathcal{R})
, a generalization of
\Pi(r)
to
multiple rewards:

\displaystyle\Pi(\mathcal{R})\coloneqq\Big\{\pi

\displaystyle\in\Pi^{\text{NM}}\,\Big|\,\exists\phi\in\Delta^{\mathcal{A}}_{\llbracket H\rrbracket\times\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{G}_{r^{1}}\times\dotsc\times\mathcal{G}_{r^{d}}}:

\displaystyle\pi(a|s,\omega)=\phi_{h}(a|s,G(\omega;r^{1}),\dotsc,G(\omega;r^{d}))\;\forall s\in\mathcal{S},a\in\mathcal{A},h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket,\omega\in\Omega_{h}\Big\}.

Intuitively,
\Pi(\mathcal{R})
contains policies that depend on the amount of rewards
collected so far for every possible reward
r^{i}
in
\mathcal{R}
.

Now, let us define
\pi_{\mathcal{R}}\in\Pi(\mathcal{R})
, an analogous of policy
\pi_{r}
(Eq.

6
).
For any set of rewards
\mathcal{R}
and expert policy
\pi^{E}\in\Pi^{\text{NM}}
, define

\pi_{\mathcal{R}}\in\Pi(\mathcal{R})
as the policy whose probability of taking an action
a
in
state
s
with history
\omega\in\Omega_{h}
coincides with the “average”
probability with which
\pi^{E}
selects
a
in
s
after accumulating

G(\omega;r^{i})
reward for each reward
r^{i}\in\mathcal{R}
:

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathcal{R}}(a|s,\omega)\coloneqq\frac{\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(s_{h}=s,\;a_{h}=a,\;G^{1}_{h}=G(\omega;r^{1}),\;\dotsc,\;G^{d}_{h}=G(\omega;r^{d}))}{\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(s_{h}=s,\;G^{1}_{h}=G(\omega;r^{1}),\;\dotsc,\;G^{d}_{h}=G(\omega;r^{d}))},

(21)

where we defined the random return at stage
h
under reward
r^{i}\in\mathcal{R}
as

G_{h}^{i}\coloneqq\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{h-1}r_{h^{\prime}}^{i}(s_{h^{\prime}},a_{h^{\prime}})
.
If the denominator is zero, we set
\pi_{r}(a|s,\omega)=1/A
.

We have the following result replicating Lemma
4.1
:

Lemma C.5
.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be any MDP
\setminus
R ,
\mathcal{R}=\{r^{1},\dotsc,r^{d}\}
any set of
d\geq 1
rewards
containing the unknown
r^{E}
, and
\pi^{E}\in\Pi^{\text{NM}}
be any policy.
Then, the policy
\pi_{\mathcal{R}}\in\Pi(\mathcal{R})
is a minimizer of Eq. (
20
), and satisfies
\eta^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}}}_{r^{i}}(g)=\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{i}}(g)

for all
g
.

We prove it in Appendix
C.4.1
.
However,
\Pi(\mathcal{R})=\Pi^{\text{NM}}
of course for some rewards. Thus, we can
discretize, by defining:

\mathcal{R}^{\theta}\coloneqq\{r^{1}_{\theta},\dotsc,r^{d}_{\theta}\}
, i.e., by discretizing
each reward inside
\mathcal{R}
. Then, it should be clear that the memory required for
storing a policy in
\Pi(\mathcal{R}^{\theta})
scales as
\mathcal{O}(SAH|\mathcal{Y}^{\theta}|^{d})

(because we do the cartesian product).
Analogously to Lemma
4.2
, we can bound the approximation
error (proof in Appendix
C.4.1
):

Lemma C.6
.

Let
\theta\in(0,1]
.
Let
\mathcal{M}
be any MDP
\setminus
R ,
\mathcal{R}=\{r^{1},\dotsc,r^{d}\}
any set of
d\geq 1
rewards
containing the unknown
r^{E}
, and
\pi^{E}\in\Pi^{\text{NM}}
be any policy.
Then, the policy
\pi_{\mathcal{R}^{\theta}}\in\Pi(\mathcal{R}^{\theta})
satisfies

\mathcal{W}\big(\eta^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}^{\theta}}}_{r^{E}},\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{E}}\big)\leq H\theta
.

Now, we address this problem with a variant of
RS
-
BC
for the no-interaction
setting, and a variant of
RS
-
KT
for the known-transition setting.

1

Input :
Dataset
\mathcal{D}^{E}

=\{(s_{1}^{i},a_{1}^{i},\dotsc,s_{H}^{i},a_{H}^{i})\}_{i\in\llbracket N\rrbracket}
, set of rewards

\mathcal{R}=\{r^{1},\dotsc,r^{d}\}
, parameter
\theta

2

//
Count the state-action-cumulative reward occurrences

3

M_{h}(s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d},a)\leftarrow\sum_{i\in\llbracket N\rrbracket}\mathds{1}\{s_{h}^{i}=s,a_{h}^{i}=a,\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{h-1}r^{1}_{\theta,h^{\prime}}(s_{h^{\prime}}^{i},a_{h^{\prime}}^{i})=g^{1},\dotsc,\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{h-1}r^{d}_{\theta,h^{\prime}}(s_{h^{\prime}}^{i},a_{h^{\prime}}^{i})=g^{d}\}

\forall h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket,s\in\mathcal{S},g^{1}\in\mathcal{Y}^{\theta}_{h},\dotsc,g^{d}\in\mathcal{Y}^{\theta}_{h},a\in\mathcal{A}

4

//
Retrieve the policy

5

\widehat{\pi}(a|s,\omega)\leftarrow\begin{cases}\frac{M_{h}(s,G(\omega;r_{\theta}^{1}),\dotsc,G(\omega;r_{\theta}^{d}),a)}{\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,G(\omega;r_{\theta}^{1}),\dotsc,G(\omega;r_{\theta}^{d}),a^{\prime})}&\text{if denominator}>0\\
\frac{1}{A}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\quad{\forall h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket,s\in\mathcal{S},\omega\in\Omega_{h},a\in\mathcal{A}}

6

Return

\widehat{\pi}

7

Algorithm 3

Variant of
RS
-
BC
for a set of rewards

We begin with a variant of
RS
-
BC
, reported in Algorithm
3
.
Simply, we count the (discretized) occurrences for every reward in
\mathcal{R}
.
We have the following result (proof in Appendix
C.4.2
):

Theorem C.7
.

Let
\epsilon\in(0,H]
and
\delta\in(0,1)
.
Let
\mathcal{M}
be any MDP
\setminus
R ,
\mathcal{R}=\{r^{1},\dotsc,r^{d}\}
any set of
d\geq 1
rewards
containing the unknown
r^{E}
, and
\pi^{E}\in\Pi^{\text{NM}}
be any policy.
Then, choosing
\theta=\epsilon/(4H)
, with probability at least
1-\delta
, the
policy
\widehat{\pi}
output by Algorithm
3
satisfies

\mathcal{W}(\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{E}},\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r^{E}})\leq\epsilon
,
with a number of samples:

\displaystyle N\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\bigg(\frac{SH^{4+2d}d^{2}\ln\frac{1}{\delta}}{\epsilon^{2+d}}\Big(A+\ln\frac{1}{\delta}\Big)\bigg).

(22)

Observe that, for
d=1
, we retrieve the known-reward setting, and the number of
samples in Eq. (
22
) matches that of
RS
-
BC
(Eq.

8
).

1

Input :
Dataset
\mathcal{D}^{E}

=\{(s_{1}^{i},a_{1}^{i},\dotsc,s_{H}^{i},a_{H}^{i})\}_{i\in\llbracket N\rrbracket}
, set of rewards

\mathcal{R}=\{r^{1},\dotsc,r^{d}\}
,
parameter
\theta
, transition model
p

2

//
Estimate the return distribution of the expert
\eta_{r}^{\pi^{E}}

for any
r\in\mathcal{R}

3

\widehat{\eta}_{r}(g)\leftarrow\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in\llbracket N\rrbracket}\mathds{1}\{\sum_{h=1}^{H}r_{\theta,h}(s_{h}^{i},a_{h}^{i})=g\}

\forall g\in\mathcal{Y}^{\theta}
,
\forall r\in\mathcal{R}

4

//
Compute the policy in
\Pi(\mathcal{R}^{\theta})
closest to

\widehat{\eta}_{r}\forall r\in\mathcal{R}
via Eq. (
24
)

5

\widehat{\pi}\in\argmin\nolimits_{\pi\in\Pi(\mathcal{R}^{\theta})}\max\nolimits_{r\in\mathcal{R}}\mathcal{W}\big(\eta^{\pi}_{r_{\theta}},\widehat{\eta}\big)

6

Return

\widehat{\pi}

7

Algorithm 4

Variant of
RS
-
KT
for a set of rewards

Now, we do the same for
RS
-
KT
. See Algorithm
4
for a variant
of the algorithm. We have (proof in Appendix
C.4.3
):

Theorem C.8
.

Let
\epsilon\in(0,H]
and
\delta\in(0,1)
.
Let
\mathcal{M}
be any MDP
\setminus
R ,
\mathcal{R}=\{r^{1},\dotsc,r^{d}\}
any set of
d\geq 1
rewards
containing the unknown
r^{E}
, and
\pi^{E}\in\Pi^{\text{NM}}
be any policy.
Assume that the optimization problem in
Line
4
is solved exactly.
Then, choosing
\theta=\epsilon/(4H)
, with probability
1-\delta
, the policy

\widehat{\pi}
output by Algorithm
4
satisfies

\mathcal{W}(\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{E}},\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r^{E}})\leq\epsilon
,
with:

\displaystyle N\leq\mathcal{O}\bigg(\frac{H^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}}\ln\frac{d}{\delta}\bigg).

(23)

Interestingly, the bound here is still polynomial.
Now, we show an extension of the LP formulation in Eq. (
10
) for addressing Line
4
of Algorithm

4
.
Specifically, we just construct an augmented MDP that keeps track of the past
rewards for every possible reward in
\mathcal{R}
, and note that
\Pi(\mathcal{R}^{\theta})

describes the set of Markovian policies in this MDP.
So, we want to match a sort of augmented return distribution for this problem:

\displaystyle\min_{d\in\mathcal{K},\eta^{1}\in\Delta^{(\mathcal{Y}^{\theta})},\dotsc,\eta^{d}\in\Delta^{(\mathcal{Y}^{\theta})}}\max\limits_{r^{i}\in\mathcal{R}}\mathcal{W}\!\left(\eta^{i},\widehat{\eta}_{r^{i}}\right)

(24)

\displaystyle\quad\text{s.t. }\;\eta^{i}(g)=\sum_{s,a,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{i-1},g^{i+1},\dotsc,g^{d}}d_{H}(s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{i-1},g-r_{\theta,H}^{i}(s,a),g^{i+1},\dotsc,g^{d},a)

(25)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\forall g\in\mathcal{Y}^{\theta}\forall i\in\llbracket d\rrbracket.

(26)

Intuitively, the constraints above enforce that
\eta^{i}
is the return
distribution induced by the occupancy measure
d
w.r.t. the reward

r^{i}_{\theta}
, for all the rewards
r^{i}\in\mathcal{R}
, and
\mathcal{K}
denotes the set of
feasible occupancy measures in this augmented MDP
(
Puterman 1994
)
:

\displaystyle\scalebox{0.95}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{K}\coloneqq\Big\{d\in\Delta_{\llbracket H\rrbracket}^{\overline{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A}}\,\Big|\,\sum_{a}d_{1}(\overline{s}_{0},a)=1\wedge\forall\overline{s}\in\overline{\mathcal{S}},h\geq 2:\;\sum_{a}d_{h}(\overline{s},a)=\sum_{\overline{s}^{\prime},a^{\prime}}d_{h-1}(\overline{s}^{\prime},a^{\prime})\overline{p}_{h-1}(\overline{s}|\overline{s}^{\prime},a^{\prime})\Big\}$},

where the state space is

\overline{\mathcal{S}}\coloneqq\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{Y}^{\theta}\times\dotsc\times\mathcal{Y}^{\theta}

d

times,
\overline{s}_{0}\coloneqq(s_{0},0,\dotsc,0)
and the transition model is:

\displaystyle p_{h}(s^{\prime},g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d}|s,\overline{g}^{1},\dotsc,\overline{g}^{d},a)\coloneqq p_{h}(s^{\prime}|s,a)\mathds{1}\{r_{h}^{1}(s,a)+\overline{g}^{1}=g^{1}\}\dotsc\mathds{1}\{r_{h}^{d}(s,a)+\overline{g}^{d}=g^{d}\}.

In words, Eq. (
24
) searches for an occupancy
measure
d\in\mathcal{K}
that induces the return distribution
\eta^{i}
closest to

\widehat{\eta}_{r^{i}}
.
From such a solution, a policy
\widehat{\pi}\in\Pi(\mathcal{R}^{\theta})
with occupancy

d^{\widehat{\pi}}=d
(and thus return distribution

\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r^{i}_{\theta}}=\eta^{i}
for all
i
) can be recovered via:

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}(a|s,\omega)=\frac{d_{h}(s,G(\omega;r_{\theta}^{1}),\dotsc,G(\omega;r_{\theta}^{d}),a)}{\sum_{a^{\prime}}d_{h}(s,G(\omega;r_{\theta}^{1}),\dotsc,G(\omega;r_{\theta}^{d}),a^{\prime})}\quad\forall h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket,\;s\in\mathcal{S},\;a\in\mathcal{A},\;\omega\in\Omega_{h},

when the denominator is nonzero, and
\widehat{\pi}(a|s,\omega)=1/A

otherwise
(
Syed et al. 2008
)
.
We remark that, being the set
\mathcal{R}
finite, then the minmax above can be
formulated as an LP minimization problem.

C.4.1
Technical Results and Proofs for the Policy Class

See
C.5

Proof.

To prove this result, we show that, for any
r^{i}\in\mathcal{R}
, the return
distribution
\eta^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}}}_{r^{i}}
coincides with the expert’s return
distribution
\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{i}}
.

For any
r^{i}\in\mathcal{R}
and
g^{\prime}\in[0,H]
, we can write (we use

G_{h}^{j}\coloneqq\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{h-1}r_{h^{\prime}}^{j}(s_{h^{\prime}},a_{h^{\prime}})
for all

j\in\llbracket d\rrbracket
):

\displaystyle\eta^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}}}_{r^{i}}(g^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}}}(G_{H}^{i}+r^{i}_{H}(s_{H},a_{H})=g^{\prime})

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(1)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{g^{i}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{i},H}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}}}(G_{H}^{i}=g^{i},s_{H}=s,r^{i}_{H}(s,a_{H})=g^{\prime}-g^{i})

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},H}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},H}}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}G_{H}^{1}=g^{1},\dotsc,G_{H}^{d}=g^{d}},s_{H}=s,r^{i}_{H}(s,a_{H})=g^{\prime}-g^{i})

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},H}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},H}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sum\limits_{a\in\mathcal{A}:r^{i}_{H}(s,a)=g^{\prime}-g^{i}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}}}(G_{H}^{1}=g^{1},\dotsc,G_{H}^{d}=g^{d},s_{H}=s,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}a_{H}=a})

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(2)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},H}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},H}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\sum\limits_{a\in\mathcal{A}:r^{i}_{H}(s,a)=g^{\prime}-g^{i}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}}}(G_{H}^{1}=g^{1},\dotsc,G_{H}^{d}=g^{d},s_{H}=s){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pi_{\mathcal{R}}(a|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})}

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(3)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},H}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},H}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\sum\limits_{a\in\mathcal{A}:r^{i}_{H}(s,a)=g^{\prime}-g^{i}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}}(G_{H}^{1}=g^{1},\dotsc,G_{H}^{d}=g^{d},s_{H}=s)\pi_{\mathcal{R}}(a|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},H}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},H}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(G_{H}^{1}=g^{1},\dotsc,G_{H}^{d}=g^{d},s_{H}=s)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\sum\limits_{a\in\mathcal{A}:r^{i}_{H}(s,a)=g^{\prime}-g^{i}}\pi_{\mathcal{R}}(a|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(4)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},H}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},H}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(G_{H}^{1}=g^{1},\dotsc,G_{H}^{d}=g^{d},s_{H}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\sum\limits_{a\in\mathcal{A}:r^{i}_{H}(s,a)=g^{\prime}-g^{i}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\frac{\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(s_{H}=s,\;a_{H}=a,\;G^{1}_{H}=g^{1},\;\dotsc,\;G^{d}_{H}=g^{d})}{\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(s_{H}=s,\;G^{1}_{H}=g^{1},\;\dotsc,\;G^{d}_{H}=g^{d})}}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},H}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},H}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\sum\limits_{a\in\mathcal{A}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathds{1}\{r^{i}_{H}(s,a)=g^{\prime}-g^{i}\}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(s_{H}=s,\;a_{H}=a,\;G^{1}_{H}=g^{1},\;\dotsc,\;G^{d}_{H}=g^{d})

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sum\limits_{g^{i}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{i},H}}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\sum\limits_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\mathds{1}\{r^{i}_{H}(s,a)=g^{\prime}-g^{i}\}\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(s_{H}=s,\;a_{H}=a,\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}G^{i}_{H}=g^{i}})

\displaystyle=\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{i}}(g^{\prime}),

where at (1) we define symbol
\mathcal{G}_{r,H}\coloneqq\{g\in[0,H-1]\,|\,\exists\omega\in\Omega_{H}:\,G(\omega;r)=g\}
for any
r
, at (2) we recognize that, by
definition,
\pi_{\mathcal{R}}
takes actions only depending on the current state, stage
and past rewards for any
r^{i}
, and we denote with brevity this fact with

\pi_{\mathcal{R}}(a|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})
, at (3) we use Lemma
C.9
, at (4) we use the definition of
\pi_{\mathcal{R}}(a|s,g)
(Eq.
21
) where the denominator is not 0, noting that, in that
case, the entire expression evaluates to 0.
∎

See
C.6

Proof.

For any reward
r^{i}\in\mathcal{R}
, we can write:

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}^{\theta}}}_{r^{i}},\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{i}}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(1)}}}{\leq}\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}^{\theta}}}_{r^{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}^{\theta}}}_{r^{i}_{\theta}}}\Big)+\mathcal{W}\Big({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}^{\theta}}}_{r^{i}_{\theta}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{i}_{\theta}}}\Big)+\mathcal{W}\Big({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{i}_{\theta}}},\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{i}}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(2)}}}{\leq}2H\|r^{i}-r^{i}_{\theta}\|_{\infty}+\mathcal{W}\Big({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}^{\theta}}}_{r^{i}_{\theta}}},{\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{i}_{\theta}}}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(3)}}}{\leq}H\theta+\mathcal{W}\Big({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}^{\theta}}}_{r^{i}_{\theta}}},{\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{i}_{\theta}}}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(4)}}}{\leq}H\theta,

where at (1) we apply twice the triangle’s inequality, at (2) we apply twice
Lemma
C.1
, at (3) we realize that, by definition
of
r^{i}_{\theta}
, it holds that
\|r^{i}-r^{i}_{\theta}\|_{\infty}\leq\theta/2
, and
finally, at (4), we apply Lemma
C.5
with
set
\mathcal{R}^{\theta}
and expert’s policy
\pi^{E}
.

The proof is concluded after having observed that
r^{E}\in\mathcal{R}
by hypothesis,
and so these passages hold also for
r^{E}
.
∎

Lemma C.9
.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be any MDP
\setminus
R ,
\mathcal{R}=\{r^{1},\dotsc,r^{d}\}
any set of
d\geq 1
rewards
containing the unknown
r^{E}
, and
\pi\in\Pi^{\text{NM}}
be any policy.
Let
\pi_{\mathcal{R}}\in\Pi(\mathcal{R})
be the policy defined as in Eq. (
21
) for expert’s policy
\pi
.
Then, for all
h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket
,
s\in\mathcal{S}
and
g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d}\in[0,h-1]
, it holds that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}}}\Big(G^{1}_{h}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G^{d}_{h}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h}=s\Big)=\mathbb{P}^{\pi}\Big(G^{1}_{h}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G^{d}_{h}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h}=s\Big),

where we used
G_{h}^{i}\coloneqq\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{h-1}r_{h^{\prime}}^{i}(s_{h^{\prime}},a_{h^{\prime}})
.

Proof.

We prove the result by induction.
Let us begin with the base case:
h=1
. For all
s\in\mathcal{S}
and

g^{,}\dotsc,g^{d}\in\{0\}^{d}
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}}}\Big(G^{1}_{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G^{d}_{1}=g^{d}\wedge s_{1}=s\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathds{1}\{g^{1}=0\wedge\dotsc\wedge g^{d}=0\}\mathds{1}\{s=s_{0}\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}^{\pi}\Big(G^{1}_{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G^{d}_{1}=g^{d}\wedge s_{1}=s\Big),

where we noticed that, for
h=1
, no action is taken yet.
Now, let us consider any stage
h\in\{2,3,\dotsc,H\}
, and let us make the
induction hypothesis that, for all
h^{\prime}\in\llbracket h-1\rrbracket
, for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
and

g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d}\in[0,h^{\prime}-1]^{d}
, it holds that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}}}\Big(G^{1}_{h^{\prime}}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G^{d}_{h^{\prime}}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h^{\prime}}=s\Big)=\mathbb{P}^{\pi}\Big(G^{1}_{h^{\prime}}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G^{d}_{h^{\prime}}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h^{\prime}}=s\Big).

Then, for any
s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}
and

\overline{g}^{1},\dotsc,\overline{g}^{d}\in[0,h-1]^{d}
, we can write:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}}}(G^{1}_{h}=\overline{g}^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G^{d}_{h}=\overline{g}^{d}\wedge s_{h}=s^{\prime})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(1)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1},(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
G(\omega;r^{i})+r_{h-1}^{i}(s,a)=\overline{g}^{i}\;\forall i\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}}}(\omega_{h-1}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s\wedge a_{h-1}=a\wedge s_{h}=s^{\prime})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(2)}}}{=}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h-1}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1}:\\
G(\omega;r^{i})=g^{i}\;\forall i\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}^{i}(s,a)=\overline{g}^{i}-g^{i}\;\forall i\end{subarray}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}}}(\omega_{h-1}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s\wedge a_{h-1}=a\wedge s_{h}=s^{\prime})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(3)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h-1}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1}:\\
G(\omega;r^{i})=g^{i}\;\forall i\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}^{i}(s,a)=\overline{g}^{i}-g^{i}\;\forall i\end{subarray}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}}}(\omega_{h-1}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\cdot\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}}}(a_{h-1}=a|\omega,s)\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}}}(s_{h}=s^{\prime}|\omega,s,a)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(4)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h-1}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1}:\\
G(\omega;r^{i})=g^{i}\;\forall i\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}^{i}(s,a)=\overline{g}^{i}-g^{i}\;\forall i\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}}}(\omega_{h-1}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}}}(a_{h-1}=a|\omega,s){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(5)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h-1}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1}:\\
G(\omega;r^{i})=g^{i}\;\forall i\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}^{i}(s,a)=\overline{g}^{i}-g^{i}\;\forall i\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}}}(\omega_{h-1}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pi_{\mathcal{R}}(a|\omega,s)}p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(6)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h-1}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h-1}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}^{i}(s,a)=\overline{g}^{i}-g^{i}\;\forall i\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1}:\\
G(\omega;r^{i})=g^{i}\;\forall i\end{subarray}}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}}}(\omega_{h-1}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\pi_{\mathcal{R}}(a|\omega,s)p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(7)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h-1}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}^{i}(s,a)=\overline{g}^{i}-g^{i}\;\forall i\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pi_{\mathcal{R}}(a|g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d},s)p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1}:\\
G(\omega;r^{i})=g^{i}\;\forall i\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}}}(\omega_{h-1}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h-1}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}^{i}(s,a)=\overline{g}^{i}-g^{i}\;\forall i\end{subarray}}\pi_{\mathcal{R}}(a|g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d},s)p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}}}(G_{h-1}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h-1}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h-1}=s)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(8)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h-1}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}^{i}(s,a)=\overline{g}^{i}-g^{i}\;\forall i\end{subarray}}\pi_{\mathcal{R}}(a|g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d},s)p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}}(G_{h-1}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h-1}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h-1}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(9)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h-1}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}^{i}(s,a)=\overline{g}^{i}-g^{i}\;\forall i\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\frac{\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h-1}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h-1}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h-1}=s\wedge a_{h-1}=a)}{\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h-1}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h-1}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h-1}=s)}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h-1}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h-1}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h-1}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h-1}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}^{i}(s,a)=\overline{g}^{i}-g^{i}\;\forall i\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h-1}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h-1}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h-1}=s\wedge a_{h-1}=a)p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h-1}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}^{i}(s,a)=\overline{g}^{i}-g^{i}\;\forall i\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h-1}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h-1}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h-1}=s\wedge a_{h-1}=a\wedge s_{h}=s^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h}^{1}=\overline{g}^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h}^{d}=\overline{g}^{d}\wedge s_{h}=s^{\prime}),

where at (1) we use symbol
\omega_{h^{\prime\prime}}
to denote the random trajectory long

h^{\prime\prime}
stages, i.e., whose realizations belong to
\Omega_{h^{\prime\prime}}
, for any

h^{\prime\prime}\in\llbracket H\rrbracket
.
At (2) we define symbols
\mathcal{G}_{r,h}\coloneqq\{g\in[0,h-1]\,|\,\exists\omega\in\Omega_{h}:\,G(\omega;r)=g\}
for any
r
,
at (3) we use the chain rule of conditional probabilities, at (4) we use the
Markovianity of the environment, at (5) we note that

\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}}}(a_{h-1}=a|\omega,s)
actually is
\pi_{\mathcal{R}}(a|\omega,s)
, at (6) we
exchange the two summations, at (7) we recognize that, by definition,

\pi_{\mathcal{R}}(a|\omega,s)
takes on the same value for all the trajectories
\omega

with the same value of return for all rewards
r^{i}\in\mathcal{R}
, and thus we can bring
this quantity outside the summation over the
\omega
. We use symbol

\pi_{\mathcal{R}}(a|g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d},s)
to denote this fact for brevity. We do the same
also for
p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)
.
At (8) we use the induction hypothesis, at (9) we replace
\pi_{\mathcal{R}}(a|g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d},s)
with
its definition when
\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h-1}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge G_{h-1}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h-1}=s)>0
as in
the opposite case the entire formula takes on value zero.
∎

C.4.2
Proof of Theorem
C.7

See
C.7

Proof.
