We can write:

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{E}},\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r^{E}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(1)}}}{\leq}\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{E}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}^{\theta}}}_{r^{E}}}\Big)+\mathcal{W}\Big({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}^{\theta}}}_{r^{E}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}^{\theta}}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}}}\Big)+\mathcal{W}\Big({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}^{\theta}}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}}}\Big)+\mathcal{W}\Big({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}}},\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r^{E}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(2)}}}{\leq}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}2H\theta}+\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}^{\theta}}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}},\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(3)}}}{\leq}2H\theta+{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}H\Big\|}\eta^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}^{\theta}}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}}-\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r^{E}_{\theta}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Big\|_{1}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(4)}}}{\leq}2H\theta+H\sum\limits_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1}_{\theta},h}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d}_{\theta},h}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{\mathcal{R}^{\theta}}}(G_{h}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\Big\|\pi_{\mathcal{R}^{\theta},h}(\cdot|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})-\widehat{\pi}_{h}(\cdot|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})\Big\|_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(5)}}}{=}2H\theta+H\sum\limits_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1}_{\theta},h}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d}_{\theta},h}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}}(G_{h}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\Big\|\pi_{\mathcal{R}^{\theta},h}(\cdot|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})-\widehat{\pi}_{h}(\cdot|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})\Big\|_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(6)}}}{\leq}2H\theta+H\epsilon^{\prime},

where at (1) we apply triangle’s inequality, at (2) we apply Lemma
C.6
and Lemma
C.1
twice, at (3) we use
Particular Case 6.13 of
Villani 2008
, which tells us that we can
upper bound the Wasserstein distance between two distributions supported on set

\mathcal{X}
by the diameter of
\mathcal{X}
(
\max_{x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}}|x-x^{\prime}|
) times the one norm
between the two distributions. Since
\mathcal{X}=[0,H]
in our case, we get the
expression written above.
At (4) we apply Lemma
C.10
with the notation
defined in that lemma with set
\mathcal{R}_{\theta}
and policies
\pi_{\mathcal{R}^{\theta}}
and

\widehat{\pi}
. Moreover, this holds recalling that
r^{E}\in\mathcal{R}
and so

r^{E}_{\theta}\in\mathcal{R}^{\theta}
.
At (5) we use Lemma
C.9
.
Lastly, at (6) we apply Lemma
C.11
with accuracy

\epsilon^{\prime}
.

The result follows by imposing that
\epsilon^{\prime}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2H}
and

2H\theta\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}
, which can be achieved by taking

\epsilon^{\prime}=\frac{\epsilon}{2H}
and
\theta=\frac{\epsilon}{4H}
, and by
observing that:

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{G}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}\prod_{i\in\llbracket d\rrbracket}|\mathcal{G}_{r^{i}_{\theta},h}|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}\prod_{i\in\llbracket d\rrbracket}|\mathcal{Y}^{\theta}_{h}|

\displaystyle=\sum_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}|\mathcal{Y}^{\theta}_{h}|^{d}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}(1+(h-1)/\theta)^{d}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{O}\Big(H(H/\theta)^{d}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(10)}}}{=}\mathcal{O}\Big(H(H^{2}/\epsilon)^{d}\Big),

where at (11) we used the previous choice
\theta=\frac{\epsilon}{4H}
.

Replacing into the number of samples in Lemma
C.11
(and
also
\epsilon^{\prime}=\frac{\epsilon}{2H}
) we get the result:

\displaystyle N\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\bigg(\frac{SH^{4+2d}d^{2}\ln\frac{1}{\delta}}{\epsilon^{2+d}}\Big(A+\ln\frac{1}{\delta}\Big)\bigg).

By using
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}
notation to hide logarithmic terms in

S,A,H,\frac{1}{\epsilon},\ln\frac{1}{\delta},d
, we get the result.
∎

Lemma C.10

(Error Propagation)
.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be any MDP
\setminus
R and
\mathcal{R}=\{r^{1},\dotsc,r^{d}\}
any set of
d\geq 1
rewards.
For any pair of policies
\pi,\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi^{\text{NM}}
such that, for all

h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket
,
a\in\mathcal{A}
,
s\in\mathcal{S}
and
\omega,\omega^{\prime}\in\Omega_{h}
with

G(\omega;r^{i})=G(\omega^{\prime};r^{i})

\forall i\in\llbracket d\rrbracket
:

\displaystyle\pi(a|\omega,s)=\pi(a|\omega^{\prime},s)\qquad\wedge\qquad\pi^{\prime}(a|\omega,s)=\pi^{\prime}(a|\omega^{\prime},s),

it holds that, for any
r^{i}\in\mathcal{R}
:

\displaystyle\Big\|\eta^{\pi}_{r^{i}}-\eta^{\pi^{\prime}}_{r^{i}}\Big\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\Big\|\pi_{h}(\cdot|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})-\pi_{h}^{\prime}(\cdot|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})\Big\|_{1},

where
\mathcal{G}_{r,h}\coloneqq\{g\in[0,h-1]\,|\,\exists\omega\in\Omega_{h}:\,G(\omega;r)=g\}
for any reward
r
,
G_{h}^{i}\coloneqq\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{h-1}r_{h^{\prime}}^{i}(s_{h^{\prime}},a_{h^{\prime}})
denotes the
random
return at stage
h
under
reward
r^{i}
, and
\pi_{h}(\cdot|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})
and

\pi_{h}^{\prime}(\cdot|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})
denote the unique probability with which the
policies
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
prescribe actions in
s
at
h
under any trajectory

\omega\in\Omega_{h}
with
G(\omega;r^{i})=g^{i}

\forall i\in\llbracket d\rrbracket
.

Proof.

To prove the result, we first demonstrate by induction that, for
all
h\in\llbracket 2,H\rrbracket
, it holds that:

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\Big|\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(G_{h}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h}=s)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\sum\limits_{h^{\prime}\in\llbracket h-1\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h^{\prime}}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h^{\prime}}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h^{\prime}}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h^{\prime}}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h^{\prime}}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\Big\|\pi_{h^{\prime}}(\cdot|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})-\pi_{h^{\prime}}^{\prime}(\cdot|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})\Big\|_{1}.

We begin with the base case
h=2
. We can write:

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},2}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},2}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\Big|\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{2}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{2}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{2}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(G_{2}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{2}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{2}=s)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(1)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},2}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},2}}\sum\limits_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\Big|\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{1}^{i}(s,a)=g^{i}\forall i\end{subarray}}\Big({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p(s^{\prime}|s,a)\mathds{1}\{s=s_{0}\}\pi(a|s)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-{p(s^{\prime}|s,a)\mathds{1}\{s=s_{0}\}\pi^{\prime}(a|s)}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(2)}}}{\leq}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},2}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},2}}\mathop{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sum}}\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}a\in\mathcal{A}:}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}r_{1}^{i}(s_{0},a)=g^{i}\forall i}\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}p(s^{\prime}|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s_{0}},a){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Big|}\pi(a|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s_{0}})-\pi^{\prime}(a|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s_{0}}){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Big|}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},2}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},2}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{1}^{i}(s_{0},a)=g^{i}\forall i\end{subarray}}\Big|\pi(a|s_{0})-\pi^{\prime}(a|s_{0})\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sum\limits_{a\in\mathcal{A}}}\Big|\pi(a|s_{0})-\pi^{\prime}(a|s_{0})\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\Big\|\pi(\cdot|s_{0})-\pi^{\prime}(\cdot|s_{0})\Big\|_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(3)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{h^{\prime}\in\llbracket 1\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h^{\prime}}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h^{\prime}}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h^{\prime}}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h^{\prime}}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h^{\prime}}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\Big\|\pi_{h^{\prime}}(\cdot|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})-\pi_{h^{\prime}}^{\prime}(\cdot|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})\Big\|_{1},

where at (1) we realize that in
\mathcal{M}
the initial state is always
s_{0}
, and
that the transition model is Markovian and independent of the policy, at (2) we
apply triangle’s inequality and keep only
s_{0}
because of the indicator, and at
(3) we have simply rewritten the expression in a more convenient way for proving
the result.

Now, let us consider any stage
h\in\llbracket 3,H\rrbracket
. Let us make the inductive
hypothesis that:

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h-1}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h-1}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\Big|\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h-1}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h-1}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h-1}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(G_{h-1}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h-1}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h-1}=s)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\sum\limits_{h^{\prime}\in\llbracket h-2\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h^{\prime}}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h^{\prime}}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h^{\prime}}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h^{\prime}}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h^{\prime}}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\Big\|\pi_{h^{\prime}}(\cdot|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})-\pi_{h^{\prime}}^{\prime}(\cdot|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})\Big\|_{1}.

Then, we can write (we use symbol
\omega_{h}
to denote the random trajectory

(s_{1},a_{1},\dotsc,s_{h},a_{h})
up to stage
h
):

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\Big|\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(G_{h}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h}=s)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(4)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h}}\sum\limits_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\Big|\sum\limits_{\overline{g}^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h-1}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{\overline{g}^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1}:\\
G(\omega;r^{i})=\overline{g}^{i}\forall i\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}^{i}(s,a)=g^{i}-\overline{g}^{i}\forall i\end{subarray}}\Big(

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\mathbb{P}^{\pi}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s})\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(a_{h-1}=a\wedge s_{h}=s^{\prime}|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\omega,s})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s})\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(a_{h-1}=a\wedge s_{h}=s^{\prime}|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\omega,s})\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(5)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h}}\sum\limits_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\Big|\sum\limits_{\overline{g}^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h-1}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{\overline{g}^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1}:\\
G(\omega;r^{i})=\overline{g}^{i}\forall i\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}^{i}(s,a)=g^{i}-\overline{g}^{i}\forall i\end{subarray}}\Big(

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pi(a|\omega,s)p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pi^{\prime}(a|\omega,s)p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h}}\sum\limits_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\Big|\sum\limits_{\overline{g}^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h-1}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{\overline{g}^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1}:\\
G(\omega;r^{i})=\overline{g}^{i}\forall i\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}^{i}(s,a)=g^{i}-\overline{g}^{i}\forall i\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)}\Big(\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)\pi(a|\omega,s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)\pi^{\prime}(a|\omega,s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)\pi^{\prime}(a|\omega,s)}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(6)}}}{\leq}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h}}\sum\limits_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\sum\limits_{\overline{g}^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h-1}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{\overline{g}^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1}:\\
G(\omega;r^{i})=\overline{g}^{i}\forall i\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}^{i}(s,a)=g^{i}-\overline{g}^{i}\forall i\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)\cdot\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Big|\pi(a|\omega,s)-\pi^{\prime}(a|\omega,s)\Big|}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h}}\sum\limits_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\sum\limits_{\overline{g}^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h-1}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{\overline{g}^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h-1}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Big|}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1}:\\
G(\omega;r^{i})=\overline{g}^{i}\forall i\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}^{i}(s,a)=g^{i}-\overline{g}^{i}\forall i\end{subarray}}p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)\pi^{\prime}(a|\omega,s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)-\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)}\Big){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Big|}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(7)}}}{\leq}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h}}\sum\limits_{\overline{g}^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h-1}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{\overline{g}^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1}:\\
G(\omega;r^{i})=\overline{g}^{i}\forall i\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}^{i}(s,a)=g^{i}-\overline{g}^{i}\forall i\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)\Big|\pi(a|\omega,s)-\pi^{\prime}(a|\omega,s)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h}}\sum\limits_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\sum\limits_{\overline{g}^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h-1}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{\overline{g}^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h-1}}\mathop{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sum}}\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}r_{h-1}^{i}(s,a)=g^{i}-\overline{g}^{i}\forall i}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Big|}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1}:\\
G(\omega;r^{i})=\overline{g}^{i}\forall i\end{subarray}}\pi^{\prime}(a|\omega,s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big(\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)-\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)\Big){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Big|}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(8)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{\overline{g}^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h-1}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{\overline{g}^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h-1}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\end{subarray}}\Big|\pi_{h-1}(a|s,\overline{g}^{1},\dotsc,\overline{g}^{d})-\pi_{h-1}^{\prime}(a|s,\overline{g}^{1},\dotsc,\overline{g}^{d})\Big|}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1}:\\
G(\omega;r^{i})=\overline{g}^{i}\forall i\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h}}\sum\limits_{\overline{g}^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h-1}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{\overline{g}^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}^{i}(s,a)=g^{i}-\overline{g}^{i}\forall i\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pi_{h-1}^{\prime}(a|s,\overline{g}^{1},\dotsc,\overline{g}^{d})}\Big|\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{h-1}:\\
G(\omega;r^{i})=\overline{g}^{i}\forall i\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big(\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)-\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(\omega_{h-2}=\omega\wedge s_{h-1}=s)\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sum\limits_{\overline{g}^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h-1}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{\overline{g}^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\end{subarray}}\Big|\pi_{h-1}(a|s,\overline{g}^{1},\dotsc,\overline{g}^{d})-\pi_{h-1}^{\prime}(a|s,\overline{g}^{1},\dotsc,\overline{g}^{d})\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h-1}^{1}=\overline{g}^{1}\wedge\dotsc,\wedge G_{h-1}^{d}=\overline{g}^{d}\wedge s_{h-1}=s)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h}}\sum\limits_{\overline{g}^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h-1}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{\overline{g}^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h-1}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{h-1}^{i}(s,a)=g^{i}-\overline{g}^{i}\forall i\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\pi_{h-1}^{\prime}(a|s,\overline{g}^{1},\dotsc,\overline{g}^{d})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\Big|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h-1}^{1}=\overline{g}^{1}\wedge\dotsc,\wedge G_{h-1}^{d}=\overline{g}^{d}\wedge s_{h-1}=s)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(G_{h-1}^{1}=\overline{g}^{1}\wedge\dotsc,\wedge G_{h-1}^{d}=\overline{g}^{d}\wedge s_{h-1}=s)}\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(9)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{\overline{g}^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h-1}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{\overline{g}^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h-1}}\sum\limits_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s\in\mathcal{S}}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h-1}^{1}=\overline{g}^{1}\wedge\dotsc,\wedge G_{h-1}^{d}=\overline{g}^{d}\wedge s_{h-1}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Big\|\pi_{h-1}(\cdot|s,\overline{g}^{1},\dotsc,\overline{g}^{d})-\pi_{h-1}^{\prime}(\cdot|s,\overline{g}^{1},\dotsc,\overline{g}^{d})\Big\|_{1}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sum\limits_{\overline{g}^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h-1}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{\overline{g}^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h-1}}\sum\limits_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s\in\mathcal{S}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\Big|\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h-1}^{1}=\overline{g}^{1}\wedge\dotsc,\wedge G_{h-1}^{d}=\overline{g}^{d}\wedge s_{h-1}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(G_{h-1}^{1}=\overline{g}^{1}\wedge\dotsc,\wedge G_{h-1}^{d}=\overline{g}^{d}\wedge s_{h-1}=s)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(10)}}}{\leq}\sum\limits_{\overline{g}^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h-1}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{\overline{g}^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h-1}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h-1}^{1}=\overline{g}^{1}\wedge\dotsc,\wedge G_{h-1}^{d}=\overline{g}^{d}\wedge s_{h-1}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\Big\|\pi_{h-1}(\cdot|s,\overline{g}^{1},\dotsc,\overline{g}^{d})-\pi_{h-1}^{\prime}(\cdot|s,\overline{g}^{1},\dotsc,\overline{g}^{d})\Big\|_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sum\limits_{h^{\prime}\in\llbracket h-2\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h^{\prime}}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h^{\prime}}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h^{\prime}}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h^{\prime}}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h^{\prime}}=s)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Big\|\pi_{h^{\prime}}(\cdot|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})-\pi_{h^{\prime}}^{\prime}(\cdot|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})\Big\|_{1}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad={\sum\limits_{h^{\prime}\in\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}h-1}\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h^{\prime}}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h^{\prime}}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad{\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h^{\prime}}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h^{\prime}}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h^{\prime}}=s)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad{\Big\|\pi_{h^{\prime}}(\cdot|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})-\pi_{h^{\prime}}^{\prime}(\cdot|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})\Big\|_{1}},

where at (4) we use the chain rule of conditional probabilities, at (5) we do it
again, and we recognize the policies
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
, and also that the
transition model is Markovian, at (6) we use triangle’s inequality to split the
summations and bring the absolute value inside, at (7), in the first term, we
note that
p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)
is the only term that depends on
s^{\prime}
and that it
sums to 1, while in the second term we exchange the order of two summations and
apply triangle’s inequality to bring one inside, at (8), in the first term, we
first remove the summation on
g^{i}
along with the indicator function that
forces us to consider a subset of state-action pairs, and then we exchange two
other summations and note that the policies do not depend by hypothesis on the
entire past trajectory, but just on the return so far for any
r^{i}\in\mathcal{R}
.
Instead, in the second term, we use that
\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}p_{h-1}(s^{\prime}|s,a)=1
, and
also that, by hypothesis,
\pi^{\prime}
does not depend on the entire past trajectory,
but just on
g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d}
. At (9), i.a., we use that
\sum_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h}}\dotsc\sum_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h}}\mathds{1}\{r_{h-1}^{i}(s,a)=g^{i}-\overline{g}^{i}\forall i\}=1
and that

\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\pi_{h-1}^{\prime}(a|s,\overline{g}^{1},\dotsc,\overline{g}^{d})=1
. Finally,
at (10), we apply the inductive hypothesis.

Thanks to this result, we can finally prove the claim in the lemma, using
passages analogous to those above, with the difference that we do not have the
summation over the states at the current stage (i.e.,
H+1
). For any
r^{j}\in\mathcal{R}
:

\displaystyle\Big\|\eta^{\pi}_{r^{j}}-\eta^{\pi^{\prime}}_{r^{j}}\Big\|_{1}=\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{j},H+1}}\Big|\eta^{\pi}_{r^{j}}(g)-\eta^{\pi^{\prime}}_{r^{j}}(g)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{j},H+1}}\Big|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{H+1}^{j}=g)}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(G_{H+1}^{j}=g)}\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sum\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{j},H+1}}\Big|\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},H}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},H}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\Omega_{H}:\\
G(\omega;r^{i})=g^{i}\forall i\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}:\\
r_{H}^{j}(s,a)=g-g^{j}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\Big(\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(\omega_{H-1}=\omega\wedge s_{H}=s)\pi(a|\omega,s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime}}(\omega_{H-1}=\omega\wedge s_{H}=s)\pi^{\prime}(a|\omega,s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(\omega_{H-1}=\omega\wedge s_{H}=s)\pi^{\prime}(a|\omega,s)}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(11)}}}{\leq}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},H}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},H}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{H}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc,\wedge G_{H}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{H}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\Big\|\pi_{H}(\cdot|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})-\pi_{H}^{\prime}(\cdot|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})\Big\|_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+{\sum\limits_{h^{\prime}\in\llbracket H-1\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1},h^{\prime}}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d},h^{\prime}}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad{\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(G_{h^{\prime}}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h^{\prime}}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h^{\prime}}=s)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad{\Big\|\pi_{h^{\prime}}(\cdot|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})-\pi_{h^{\prime}}^{\prime}(\cdot|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})\Big\|_{1}},

where at (11) we made the same passages as above from (6) on, all in one, with
the only differences that we sum only over
g\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{j},H+1}
instead of doing
so for any reward of
\mathcal{R}
, and also that we do not have the sum over the next
state
s^{\prime}
. The result follows by summing the two terms in the last expression
written.

This concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma C.11

(Concentration)
.

Let
\epsilon\in(0,H]
and
\delta\in(0,1)
.
Let
\mathcal{M}
be any MDP
\setminus
R and
\mathcal{R}=\{r^{1},\dotsc,r^{d}\}
any set of
d\geq 1
rewards,

\pi^{E}\in\Pi^{\text{NM}}
be any expert’s policy, and
\widehat{\pi}
be the
output of Algorithm
3
.
Then, with probability
1-\delta
, we have that (we use the notation in Lemma

C.10
):

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1}_{\theta},h}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d}_{\theta},h}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}

\displaystyle(G_{h}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h}=s)

\displaystyle\Big\|\pi_{\mathcal{R}^{\theta},h}(\cdot|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})-\widehat{\pi}_{h}(\cdot|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})\Big\|_{1}\leq\epsilon,

with a number of samples:

\displaystyle N\leq\frac{193SH\overline{\mathcal{G}}\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}}{\epsilon^{2}}\bigg(\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}+(A-1)\ln\Big(\frac{128eSH\overline{\mathcal{G}}\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}}{\epsilon^{2}}\Big)\bigg),

where
\overline{\mathcal{G}}\coloneqq\sum_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}\prod_{i\in\llbracket d\rrbracket}|\mathcal{G}_{r^{i}_{\theta},h}|
.

Proof.

We can write:

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1}_{\theta},h}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d}_{\theta},h}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(G_{h}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\Big\|\pi_{\mathcal{R}^{\theta},h}(\cdot|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})-\widehat{\pi}_{h}(\cdot|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})\Big\|_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sum\limits_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1}_{\theta},h}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d}_{\theta},h}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(G_{h}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\sum\limits_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\Big|\pi_{\mathcal{R}^{\theta},h}(a|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})-\Big(\frac{M_{h}(s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d},a)}{\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d},a^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\mathds{1}\{\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d},a^{\prime})>0\}+\frac{1}{A}\mathds{1}\{\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d},a^{\prime})=0\}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(1)}}}{\leq}\sum\limits_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1}_{\theta},h}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d}_{\theta},h}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(G_{h}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}2\sqrt{2}\sqrt{\frac{\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}+(A-1)\ln\big(e\big(1+\frac{\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d},a^{\prime})}{A-1}\big)\big)}{\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d},a^{\prime})}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(2)}}}{\leq}2\sqrt{2}\sqrt{\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}+(A-1)\ln\Big(e\Big(1+\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}N}}{A-1}\Big)\Big)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\sum\limits_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1}_{\theta},h}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d}_{\theta},h}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(G_{h}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h}=s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\sqrt{\frac{1}{\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d},a^{\prime})}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(3)}}}{\leq}2\sqrt{2}\sqrt{\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}+(A-1)\ln\Big(e\Big(1+\frac{N}{A-1}\Big)\Big)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\sum\limits_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1}_{\theta},h}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d}_{\theta},h}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\sqrt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\frac{8\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(G_{h}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h}=s)}{N}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}8\sqrt{\frac{\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}}{N}}}\sqrt{\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}+(A-1)\ln\Big(e\Big(1+\frac{N}{A-1}\Big)\Big)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\sum\limits_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1}_{\theta},h}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d}_{\theta},h}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\sqrt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(G_{h}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h}=s)}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(4)}}}{\leq}8\sqrt{\frac{\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}}{N}}\sqrt{\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}+(A-1)\ln\Big(e\Big(1+\frac{N}{A-1}\Big)\Big)}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sqrt{S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\sqrt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sum\limits_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1}_{\theta},h}}\dotsc\sum\limits_{g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d}_{\theta},h}}\sum\limits_{s\in\mathcal{S}}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(G_{h}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h}=s)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=8\sqrt{\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}}{N}}\sqrt{\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}+(A-1)\ln\Big(e\Big(1+\frac{N}{A-1}\Big)\Big)}\sqrt{\sum\limits_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}1}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(5)}}}{\leq}8\sqrt{\frac{S{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}H\overline{\mathcal{G}}}\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}}{N}}\sqrt{\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}+(A-1)\ln\Big(e\Big(1+\frac{N}{A-1}\Big)\Big)},

where at (1) we use that, if
\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d},a^{\prime})=0
, then:

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\Big|\pi_{\mathcal{R}^{\theta},h}(a|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})-\Big(\frac{M_{h}(s,g,a)}{\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d},a^{\prime})}\mathds{1}\{\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d},a^{\prime})>0\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{A}\mathds{1}\{\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d},a^{\prime})=0\}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sum\limits_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\Big|\pi_{\mathcal{R}^{\theta},h}(a|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})-\frac{1}{A}\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq 2,

as we the total variation distance between two probability distributions
cannot exceed 1. Instead, if
\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d},a^{\prime})>0
,
conditioning

on
\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d},a^{\prime})
, at all
s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d}
where
\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(G_{h}=g\wedge s_{h}=s)>0
, we note that
M_{h}(s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d},a)/\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d},a^{\prime})
is the
empirical vector of probabilities of
\pi_{\mathcal{R}^{\theta},h}(a|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})
(recall its
definition from Eq.
21
), thus we can
apply Lemma 8 of
Kaufmann et al. 2021
to get that, for any

\delta\in(0,1)
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}\Big(KL\Big(\frac{M_{h}(s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d},\cdot)}{\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d},a^{\prime})}\Big\|\pi_{\mathcal{R}^{\theta},h}(\cdot|s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d})\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\frac{\ln\frac{1}{\delta}+(A-1)\ln\big(e\big(1+\frac{\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d},a^{\prime})}{A-1}\big)\big)}{\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d},a^{\prime})}\Big)\geq 1-\delta.

Combining this result with the Pinsker’s inequality, that tells us that

\|x-y\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{2KL(x\|y)}
, and with a union bound over all

h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket
,
s\in\mathcal{S}
,

g^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{1}_{\theta},h},\dotsc,g^{d}\in\mathcal{G}_{r^{d}_{\theta},h}
, we get the passage
in (1) w.p.
1-\delta/2
. Note that we add an additional 2 for the case

\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d},a^{\prime})=0
, and we define
\overline{\mathcal{G}}\coloneqq\sum_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}\prod_{i\in\llbracket d\rrbracket}|\mathcal{G}_{r^{i}_{\theta},h}|
.
At (2) we bound
\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d},a^{\prime})\leq N
, and bring that
quantity outside, at (3) we apply Lemma A.1 of
Xie et al. 2021
, after
having noticed that
\sum_{a^{\prime}}M_{h}(s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d},a^{\prime})\sim\text{Bin}\Big(N,\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(G_{h}^{1}=g^{1}\wedge\dotsc\wedge G_{h}^{d}=g^{d}\wedge s_{h}=s)\Big)
, and make it
hold for all
s,g^{1},\dotsc,g^{d},h
w.p.
1-\delta/2
.
At (4) and (5) we apply the Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality.

Now, we impose that this quantity is smaller than
\epsilon
:

\displaystyle 8\sqrt{\frac{SH\overline{\mathcal{G}}\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}}{N}}\sqrt{\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}+(A-1)\ln\Big(e\Big(1+\frac{N}{A-1}\Big)\Big)}\leq\epsilon

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\iff N\geq\frac{64SH\overline{\mathcal{G}}\ln^{2}\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}}{\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{64SH\overline{\mathcal{G}}(A-1)\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}}{\epsilon^{2}}\ln\Big(\frac{eN}{A-1}+e\Big).

Thanks to Lemma J.3 of
Lazzati et al. 2024
, we know that this inequality
is satisfied with:

\displaystyle N\leq\frac{128SH\overline{\mathcal{G}}\ln^{2}\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}}{\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{192SH\overline{\mathcal{G}}(A-1)\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}}{\epsilon^{2}}\ln\Big(\frac{128eSH\overline{\mathcal{G}}\ln\frac{2S\overline{\mathcal{G}}}{\delta}}{\epsilon^{2}}\Big)+A-1.

Rearranging and applying a final union bound concludes the proof.
∎

C.4.3
Proof of Theorem
C.8

See
C.8

Proof.

Observe that, in the same way as in the proof of Theorem
4.4
or

5.1
, it is simple to see that:

\displaystyle\max\limits_{r\in\mathcal{R}}\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r},\widehat{\eta}_{r}\Big)\leq H\theta/2+H\epsilon^{\prime},

with probability
1-\delta
, by using:

\displaystyle N\leq\frac{1}{2(\epsilon^{\prime})^{2}}\ln\frac{2d}{\delta},

data, where we made a union bound over the
d
rewards in
\mathcal{R}^{\theta}
.
Then, conditioning on this event and proceeding as in the proof of Theorem

5.2
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{E}},\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r^{E}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\max\limits_{r\in\mathcal{R}}\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r},\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(1)}}}{\leq}\max\limits_{r\in\mathcal{R}}\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\widehat{\eta}_{r}}\Big)+\max\limits_{r\in\mathcal{R}}\mathcal{W}\Big({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\widehat{\eta}_{r}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r_{\theta}}}\Big)+\max\limits_{r\in\mathcal{R}}\mathcal{W}\Big({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r_{\theta}}},\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(2)}}}{\leq}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}H\epsilon^{\prime}+H\theta}+\max\limits_{r\in\mathcal{R}}\mathcal{W}\Big(\widehat{\eta}_{r},\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r_{\theta}}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(3)}}}{=}H\epsilon^{\prime}+H\theta+{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\min\limits_{\pi\in\Pi(\mathcal{R}^{\theta})}}\max\limits_{r\in\mathcal{R}}\mathcal{W}\Big(\widehat{\eta}_{r},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\pi}_{r_{\theta}}}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(4)}}}{\leq}H\epsilon^{\prime}+H\theta+\max\limits_{r\in\mathcal{R}}\mathcal{W}\Big(\widehat{\eta}_{r},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r_{\theta}}}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(5)}}}{\leq}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}2H\epsilon^{\prime}+2H\theta},

where at (1) we use triangle’s inequality, at (2) we use that event above holds
and Lemma
C.1
, at (3) we use the definition of

\widehat{\pi}
, at (4) we upper bound the minimum with a specific choice of
reward in
\Pi^{\text{NM}}
, i.e.,
\pi^{E}
, and finally, at (5), we apply again
that event holds and Lemma
C.1
.

If we now choose
\theta=\epsilon/(4H)
, and
\epsilon^{\prime}=\epsilon/(4H)
, we get that,
with probability
1-\delta
, the claim of the theorem holds with data:

\displaystyle N\leq\frac{8H^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}}\ln\frac{2d}{\delta}.

∎

C.5
When
r^{E}
is linear in a known feature map

In this appendix, we consider a variant of the known-reward setting, in which

r^{E}
is unknown, but we have knowledge of a
d-
dimensional feature map

\phi:\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\to[-1,+1]^{d}
such that the expert reward
r^{E}
can be written as:

\displaystyle r^{E}_{h}(s,a)=\langle\phi(s,a),w_{h}\rangle=\sum\limits_{i\in\llbracket d\rrbracket}\phi_{i}(s,a)w_{h,i},

for some unknown vectors
w_{h}\in[-1,+1]^{d}
. Note that the linear reward setting
is common in the IL literature
(
Abbeel & Ng 2004
)
.
We consider the following robust variant of RDM for this setting:

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}\in\argmin_{\pi\in\Pi^{\text{NM}}}\max\limits_{w:\llbracket H\rrbracket\to[-1,+1]^{d}}\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta_{\phi w}^{\pi},\eta_{\phi w}^{\pi^{E}}\Big),

(27)

where notation
\phi w
denotes the reward obtained through the dot product
between
\phi
and
w
.

We now sketch how this setting can be easily addressed through the technique
presented in Appendix
C.4
.

First, consider each
known
feature
\phi^{i}:\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\to[-1,+1]
as a reward
function, and define the set of “rewards”

\Phi\coloneqq\{\phi^{1},\dotsc,\phi^{d}\}
. Then, consider the set of policies

\Pi(\Phi)
using definition in Appendix
C.4
, and let

\pi_{\Phi}
be the policy defined as in Eq. (
21
). Then, from Lemma
C.5
,
7
7

7

Modulo some slight difference as rewards are in
[0,1]
but
features are in
[-1,+1]
.
we have the guarantee that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{\Phi}}\Big(\sum\limits_{h=1}^{H}\phi^{i}(s_{h},a_{h})=g\Big)=\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}\Big(\sum\limits_{h=1}^{H}\phi^{i}(s_{h},a_{h})=g\Big)\qquad\forall g\in[-1,+1],\forall i\in\llbracket d\rrbracket.

As a consequence, we have that, for any
w_{h}\in[-1,+1]^{d}
, it holds that:

\displaystyle\eta^{\pi_{\Phi}}_{\phi w}(g)=\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{\phi w}(g)\qquad\forall g,

since the cumulative feature map collected is the same.
Simply put, this means that set
\Pi(\Phi)
(and also policy
\pi_{\Phi}
) suffice
for this “linear” variant of the RDM problem. However, as the feature map is
arbitrary,
\Pi(\Phi)
might be too large. Thus, we may want to discretize.

Extend the discretization approach of Section
4.1
to
“rewards” in
[-1,+1]
and define set

\Phi^{\theta}\coloneqq\{\phi^{1}_{\theta},\dotsc,\phi^{d}_{\theta}\}
.
Then, observe that the policy
\pi_{\Phi^{\theta}}
satisfies a variant of Lemma

C.6
:

\displaystyle\max\limits_{w:\llbracket H\rrbracket\to[-1,+1]^{d}}\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta_{\phi w}^{\pi_{\Phi^{\theta}}},\eta_{\phi w}^{\pi^{E}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(1)}}}{\leq}\max\limits_{w:\llbracket H\rrbracket\to[-1,+1]^{d}}\bigg(\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta_{\phi w}^{\pi_{\Phi^{\theta}}},\eta_{\phi_{\theta}w}^{\pi_{\Phi^{\theta}}}\Big)+\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta_{\phi_{\theta}w}^{\pi_{\Phi^{\theta}}},\eta_{\phi_{\theta}w}^{\pi^{E}}\Big)+\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta_{\phi_{\theta}w}^{\pi^{E}},\eta_{\phi_{w}}^{\pi^{E}}\Big)\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(2)}}}{\leq}2dH^{2}\theta+\max\limits_{w:\llbracket H\rrbracket\to[-1,+1]^{d}}\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta_{\phi_{\theta}w}^{\pi_{\Phi^{\theta}}},\eta_{\phi_{\theta}w}^{\pi^{E}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(3)}}}{=}2dH^{2}\theta,

where at (1) we use triangle’s inequality and denote
\phi_{\theta}
the feature
map which is discretized in each dimension, at (2) we apply Lemma
C.1
twice
8
8

8

We upper bound with an additional factor of 2
to keep into account that now rewards are in
[-1,+1]
.
and observe that,
using Holder’s inequality and the definition of discretization:
\max_{w}\|\phi w-\phi_{\theta}w\|_{\infty}\leq\max_{\phi}\|\phi-\phi_{\theta}\|_{1}\leq d\max_{\phi}\|\phi-\phi_{\theta}\|_{\infty}\leq dH\theta/2
, and at (3) we use the
aforementioned property (i.e., Lemma
C.5
).

Therefore, the policy
\pi_{\Phi^{\theta}}
, and so the set
\Pi(\Phi^{\theta})
,
suffice for this new robust RDM problem. It is immediate to extend
RS
-
BC
and

RS
-
KT
to this setting by simply extending Algorithms
3
and

4
using input rewards taking values in
[-1,+1]
, and we are
done. Then, by adjusting Theorems
C.7
and
C.8
to
keep track of this small variation, we can have also theoretical guarantees.
Specifically, for the number of samples in Eqs. (
22
) and (
23
), we can guarantee that the
policy output by the newly constructed algorithms has a return distribution
close to that of
\eta_{\phi w}^{\pi_{\Phi^{\theta}}}
for any
w
, which in turn
has a return distribution close to that of the expert for any
w
as shown
above. To do this for the variant of
RS
-
BC
, note that we just need to extend
the proof of Lemma
C.10
. The crucial insight in
doing so is that both
\pi_{\Phi^{\theta}}
and our estimate
\widehat{\pi}

play actions with same probability at all trajectories with the same cumulative
discretized features. Regarding the variant of
RS
-
KT
, the proof is immediate as
we just need a union bound over all the features.

C.6
Generalization to Arbitrary Problems

In this appendix, we sketch how to extend
RS
-
BC
and its analysis to arbitrary IL
problems of the following kind, in which we aim to find a policy
\widehat{\pi}

that minimizes:

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}\in\argmin\limits_{\pi\in\Pi^{\text{NM}}}\sum\limits_{h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket}\sum\limits_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{h}}\Big|\mathbb{P}^{\pi}(\omega_{h}\in x)-\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(\omega_{h}\in x)\Big|,

where
\mathcal{X}=\{\mathcal{X}_{h}\}_{h}
is any partition of the set of trajectories satisfying a
certain property, specifically:

\displaystyle\bigcup\limits_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{h}}x=\Omega_{h+1}\qquad\forall h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket,

\displaystyle x\cap x^{\prime}=\{\}\qquad\forall x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{h},\forall h\in\llbracket H\rrbracket,

\displaystyle\forall x\in\mathcal{X}_{h},\;\forall(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A},\;\exists x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{h+1},\;\forall\omega\in x:\;\omega\cdot s\cdot a\in x^{\prime}\;\forall h\in\llbracket H-1\rrbracket,

where
\omega\cdot s\cdot a
denotes the trajectory obtained by concatenating

\omega
with
s,a
.

First, define:

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathcal{X}}(a|s,\omega)\coloneqq\frac{\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(s_{h}=s,\;a_{h}=a,\;\omega_{h-1}\in\mathcal{X}_{h-1}(\omega))}{\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(s_{h}=s,\;\omega_{h-1}\in\mathcal{X}_{h-1}(\omega))},

(28)

when the denominator is not 0, and
1/A
otherwise, and
\mathcal{X}_{h^{\prime}}(\cdot)

denotes the set of trajectories to which
\cdot
belongs. Then, this policy
satisfies:

Theorem C.12
.

It holds that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{\mathcal{X}}}(s_{h}=s\wedge\omega_{h-1}\in x)=\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(s_{h}=s\wedge\omega_{h-1}\in x)\qquad\forall h\in\llbracket H+1\rrbracket,s\in\mathcal{S},x\in\mathcal{X}_{h-1}.

Proof.

The proof follows that of Lemma
C.2
and
C.9
. Specifically, we prove it by induction. At
h=1
, note that it
trivially holds as
\mathcal{X}_{0}=\emptyset
. Now, make the induction hypothesis that at
any
h^{\prime}<h
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{\mathcal{X}}}(s_{h^{\prime}}=s\wedge\omega_{h^{\prime}-1}\in x)=\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(s_{h^{\prime}}=s\wedge\omega_{h^{\prime}-1}\in x)\qquad s\in\mathcal{S},x\in\mathcal{X}_{h^{\prime}-1}.

Then, at
h
, for any
s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}
and
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{h-1}
, we can write:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{\mathcal{X}}}(s_{h}=s^{\prime}\wedge\omega_{h-1}\in x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{s,a,\omega}\mathds{1}\{\omega\cdot s\cdot a\in x^{\prime}\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{\mathcal{X}}}(s_{h}=s^{\prime}\wedge s_{h-1}=s\wedge a_{h-1}=a\wedge\omega_{h-2}=\omega)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{h-1}}\sum\limits_{\omega\in x}\sum\limits_{s,a}\mathds{1}\{\omega\cdot s\cdot a\in x^{\prime}\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad p_{h}(s^{\prime}|s,a)\pi_{\mathcal{X}}(a|s,\omega)\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{\mathcal{X}}}(s_{h-1}=s\wedge\omega_{h-2}=\omega)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(1)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{h-1}}\sum\limits_{s,a}\mathds{1}\{x\cdot s\cdot a\in x^{\prime}\}p_{h}(s^{\prime}|s,a)\pi_{\mathcal{X}}(a|s,x)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\sum\limits_{\omega\in x}\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{\mathcal{X}}}(s_{h-1}=s\wedge\omega_{h-2}=\omega)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{h-1}}\sum\limits_{s,a}\mathds{1}\{x\cdot s\cdot a\in x^{\prime}\}p_{h}(s^{\prime}|s,a)\pi_{\mathcal{X}}(a|s,x)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{\mathcal{X}}}(s_{h-1}=s\wedge\omega_{h-2}\in x)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(2)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{h-1}}\sum\limits_{s,a}\mathds{1}\{x\cdot s\cdot a\in x^{\prime}\}p_{h}(s^{\prime}|s,a)\pi_{\mathcal{X}}(a|s,x)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(s_{h-1}=s\wedge\omega_{h-2}\in x)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(3)}}}{=}\sum\limits_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{h-1}}\sum\limits_{s,a}\mathds{1}\{x\cdot s\cdot a\in x^{\prime}\}p_{h}(s^{\prime}|s,a)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(s_{h-1}=s\wedge a_{h-1}=a\wedge\omega_{h-2}\in x)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E}}(s_{h}=s^{\prime}\wedge\omega_{h-1}\in x^{\prime}),

where at (1) we use notation
x\cdot s\cdot a
to denote that all the
trajectories in
x
when combined to a given
s,a
give birth to the same
x^{\prime}

by hypothesis, at (2) we use the induction hypothesis, and at (3) the definition
of
\pi_{\mathcal{X}}
.
∎

Then,
RS
-
BC
can be easily extended by counting the number of occurrences in each
set of trajectories, and also the theoretical guarantees can be easily extended
to this setting.

Appendix D
Additional Results and Proofs for Section
5

See
5.1

Proof.

For any reward
r:\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\times\llbracket H\rrbracket\to[0,1]
, we can write:

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta_{r}^{\pi^{E}},\widehat{\eta}_{r}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(1)}}}{\leq}\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta_{r}^{\pi^{E}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta_{r_{\theta}}^{\pi^{E}}}\Big)+\mathcal{W}\Big({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta_{r_{\theta}}^{\pi^{E}}},\widehat{\eta}_{r}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(2)}}}{\leq}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\frac{H\theta}{2}}+\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta_{r_{\theta}}^{\pi^{E}},\widehat{\eta}_{r}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(3)}}}{\leq}\frac{H\theta}{2}+H\epsilon^{\prime},

where at (1) we use triangle’s inequality, at (2) we apply Lemma
C.1
, and at (3) we use the same derivation as in the proof of
Theorem
4.4
, after having noticed that the estimate

\widehat{\eta}_{r}
is the same estimate used in Line
2
of
RS
-
KT
. So, by the DKW inequality, we have that the last
passage holds with probability
1-\delta
using a number of samples:

\displaystyle N\leq\frac{1}{2(\epsilon^{\prime})^{2}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta}.

This holds for a single reward
r:\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\times\llbracket H\rrbracket\to[0,1]
. To make this hold for any
possible reward, observe that it suffices to guarantee that it holds for all
the rewards in the set
\mathcal{R}
, defined as the set of all reward functions
taking on discretized values:

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}\coloneqq\Big\{r:\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\times\llbracket H\rrbracket\to\mathcal{Y}_{2}^{\theta}\Big\}.

Indeed,
\mathcal{R}
represents an
H\theta/2
-covering of the set of all the
real-valued reward functions. Therefore, the result follows through the
application of a union bound over all the rewards in
\mathcal{R}
. Since they are

|\mathcal{Y}_{2}^{\theta}|^{SAH}
, then we obtain a number of samples:

\displaystyle N\leq\frac{2SAH^{3}}{\epsilon^{2}}\ln\frac{2|\mathcal{Y}^{\theta}_{2}|}{\delta}\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\Big(\frac{SAH^{3}}{\epsilon^{2}}\ln\frac{1}{\delta}\Big),

to guarantee that
\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta_{r}^{\pi^{E}},\widehat{\eta}_{r}\Big)\leq\epsilon
for
all
r
, where we set
\epsilon^{\prime}=\epsilon/(2H)
,
\theta=\epsilon/H
, and

|\mathcal{Y}^{\theta}_{2}|\leq 1+1/\theta=1+H/\epsilon
.
∎

See
5.2

Proof.

Define the good event
\mathcal{E}
as:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}\coloneqq\bigg\{\max\limits_{r:\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\times\llbracket H\rrbracket\to[0,1]}\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r},\widehat{\eta}_{r}\Big)\leq\epsilon\bigg\}.

Then, under
\mathcal{E}
, it holds that:

\displaystyle\max\limits_{r:\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\times\llbracket H\rrbracket\to[0,1]}\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r},\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(1)}}}{\leq}\max\limits_{r:\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\times\llbracket H\rrbracket\to[0,1]}\mathcal{W}\Big(\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\widehat{\eta}_{r}}\Big)+\max\limits_{r:\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\times\llbracket H\rrbracket\to[0,1]}\mathcal{W}\Big({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\widehat{\eta}_{r}},\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(2)}}}{\leq}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\epsilon}+\max\limits_{r:\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\times\llbracket H\rrbracket\to[0,1]}\mathcal{W}\Big(\widehat{\eta}_{r},\eta^{\widehat{\pi}}_{r}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(3)}}}{\leq}\epsilon+{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\min\limits_{\pi\in\Pi^{\text{NM}}}}\max\limits_{r:\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\times\llbracket H\rrbracket\to[0,1]}\mathcal{W}\Big(\widehat{\eta}_{r},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\pi}_{r}}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(4)}}}{\leq}\epsilon+\max\limits_{r:\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\times\llbracket H\rrbracket\to[0,1]}\mathcal{W}\Big(\widehat{\eta}_{r},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r}}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\makebox[0.0pt]{(5)}}}{\leq}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}2\epsilon},

where at (1) we use triangle’s inequality, at (2) we use that event
\mathcal{E}
holds,
at (3) we use the definition of
\widehat{\pi}
, at (4) we upper bound the
minimum with a specific choice of reward in
\Pi^{\text{NM}}
, i.e.,
\pi^{E}
,
and finally, at (5), we apply again that event
\mathcal{E}
holds.
The proof is concluded by applying Theorem
5.1
, which shows that, with the samples in Eq. (
11
), the event
\mathcal{E}
holds w.p.
1-\delta
.
∎

Appendix E
Additional Details on Numerical Simulations

We describe how we sample an MDP and an expert’s policy in the experiments in
Appendix
E.1
. In Appendix
E.2
, we discuss the (im)possibility of implementing a variant of
RS
-
KT
that works with Markovian policies, in Appendix
E.3

we provide implementation details on
MIMIC-MD
, in Appendix
E.4
we
provide a graphical example of expert’s return distribution and its estimates
from the four algorithms considered in Section
6
, and,
finally, in Appendices
E.5
and
E.6
we
provide additional details and results on the simulations conducted to address,
respectively, questions 1 and 2. We mention that all simulations took place in
some hours on a AMD Ryzen 5 5500U processor.

E.1
Details on Sampling the MDPs and the Expert’s Policies

All the MDPs are sampled as follows. The initial state
s_{0}\in\mathcal{S}
is sampled
uniformly at random from
\mathcal{S}
. The reward function
r^{E}:\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\times\llbracket H\rrbracket\to[0,1]
is
sampled, in each
s,a,h
, uniformly at random from a set

\{0,\rho,2\rho,\dotsc,\lfloor 1/\rho\rfloor\rho\}
, whose values are controlled by a
parameter
\rho\in(0,1]
(intuitively, the difference
\theta-\rho
gives
insights into the approximation error).
The transition model
p
is obtained in two steps. First, we sample it uniformly
at random in each
s,a,h
from the simplex
\Delta^{\mathcal{S}}
(by sampling from the
Dirichlet distribution), but then we make the transition of each
s,a,h

deterministic with probability 0.7 to increase the variety of the MDP.

Regarding the expert’s policy, when we sample Markovian expert’s policies, we
simply sample them uniformly from the simplex
\Delta^{\mathcal{A}}
in every

\mathcal{S}\times\llbracket H\rrbracket
. Instead, when we mention “non-Markovian” expert’s
policies, then, of course, we cannot sample them uniformly at random from

\Pi^{\text{NM}}
due to the curse of dimensionality. Instead, what we do is
sample randomly from a parameteric subset of
\Pi^{\text{NM}}
defined as
follows. We map states
\{s_{1},\dotsc,s_{S}\}=\mathcal{S}
to
\{0,\dotsc,S-1\}
, and
actions
\{a_{1},\dotsc,a_{A}\}=\mathcal{A}
to
\{0,\dotsc,A-1\}
. Then, each policy
projects each past history
(s_{1},a_{1},s_{2},a_{2},\dotsc,s_{h},a_{h})
to
\mathbb{R}^{16}

after having mapped it to integers and padded with zeros to reach size
2H
, by
using a projection matrix of size
(2H,16)
randomly generated.
Then, we obtain the probabilities of playing each action by multiplying such
16-dimensional vector by a matrix of weigths of size
(16,A)
corresponding to
the current state
s_{h}
(we randomly generated one of these weight matrices for
every state).

E.2
On Addressing Return Distribution Matching with Markovian Policies

We mention that finding the best Markovian policy
\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi^{\text{M}}
with
return distribution closest to a given array
\eta
, i.e., addressing

\min_{\pi\in\Pi^{\text{M}}}\mathcal{W}(\eta_{r^{E}_{\theta}}^{\pi},\eta)
, seems a problem
that cannot be formulated as an LP and not even as a more general convex
optimization problem, as it seems to require bilinear constraints. If so,
generating random MDPs and (non-Markovian) expert policies to understand how
much Markovian policies are outperformed by non-Markovian policies for return
distribution matching may be difficult. We believe that understanding more
in-depth this fact will be interesting for future works.

E.3
Implementation Details of MIMIC-MD

Algorithm
MIMIC-MD
has been devised by

Rajaraman et al. 2020
but an efficient LP
formulation is provided in
Rajaraman et al. 2021

(see its proof of Theorem 2). Although it concerns only deterministic expert’s
policies, we extend it to stochastic policies as in the full algorithm as
follows.
Simply, in addition to the constraints for
d
being a valid occupancy measure,
we replace constraint
d_{h}(s,a)=\mathds{1}\{a=\pi^{E}_{h}(s)\}
(valid for deterministic
policies) with
d_{h}(s,a)=\frac{N_{h}(s,a)}{N_{h}(s)}\sum_{a^{\prime}}d_{h}(s,a^{\prime})
where

N_{h}(s)\neq 0
to extend to stochatic experts. Note that it is linear in
d
.

E.4
An Example of Expert’s Return Distribution and its Estimates

In Figure
3
, we plot the (discretized) return distribution
of the expert’s policy
\eta^{\pi^{E}}_{r^{E}}
(in blue,
\eta^{E}
) obtained after
having sampled at random an MDP with size
S,A,H=(3,2,8)
and the policy as
well. We discretized it with bins at a distance of
0.1
for computation and
plotting.

Then, we have sampled a dataset
\mathcal{D}^{E}
of
N=300
trajectories from
\pi^{E}
,
and computed an estimate of
\eta^{E}
as in Line
2
of Alg.
2
using
\theta=0.05
(in
green,
\widehat{\eta}
). Note that
\widehat{\eta}
is quite close to

\eta^{E}
with this amount of trajectories.

We have then given
\mathcal{D}^{E}
(and potentially the transition model) in input to
the four algorithms considered in Section
6
, i.e.,

RS
-
BC
,
RS
-
KT
,
BC
and
MIMIC-MD
, obtaining the return distributions

\eta^{\text{RS-BC}}
,
\eta^{\text{RS-KT}}
,
\eta^{\text{BC}}
and

\eta^{\text{MIMIC-MD}}
.

Observe that
RS
-
BC
tends to approximate
\eta^{E}
directly, while
RS
-
KT
tries to
match
\widehat{\eta}
instead. Anyway, both return distributions are close to

\eta^{E}
. Instead,
BC
and
MIMIC-MD
, by working with Markovian policies, are
biased, and their return distributions are not that close to
\eta^{E}
.

Figure 3:
An example of expert’r return distribution and various estimates
computed with
RS
-
BC
,
RS
-
KT
,
BC
and
MIMIC-MD
.

Table 3:
Results of simulation with
S,A,H=(2,2,20)
for Q1.

For simulations, we set
\rho=0.03<\theta
, meaning that there is some
approximation error.

Regarding Table
2
, we would like to discuss some points. First,
we mention that the increase in size of
S
and
A
is not sufficiently big to
permit to
RS
-
KT
to outperform the sample complexity of
RS
-
BC
, as discussed in
Q4.
Second, increasing
S,A,H
makes
RS
-
KT
much more time-consuming, as it requires
solving an LP with much more variables and constraints.
Third,
RS
-
KT
performs comparably to
BC
and
MIMIC-MD
, but this is due to an
increment of approximation error due to the increase of
S,A
, as clear from
Table
5
, where in absence of approximation error
RS
-
KT
outperforms
BC
and
MIMIC-MD
. Note that this is not the fact for
RS
-
BC
, which
seems more robust to approximation error for this problem size (intuitively, the
reason is that it is strictly more expressive than
BC
for any choice of

\theta
).

Regarding Table
3
, we mention that a larger
H
implies a
larger approximation error in particular for
RS
-
KT
, as clear from Table

6
, where in absence of approximation error
RS
-
KT
outperforms

BC
and
MIMIC-MD
.

The three additional simulations have all a non-Markovian expert and

\rho=\theta
(to enforce no approximation error) with parameters

S,A,H,\theta\in\{(2,2,5,5e-2),(20,3,5,5e-2),(2,2,20,1e-1)\}
, and the results
are reported respectively in Tables
4
,
5
and

6
.

By comparing these tables respectively with Table
1
(top),

2
and
3
, where there is approximation error
due to
\theta=0.05>0.03=\rho
, we realize that the approximation error mostly
concerns
RS
-
KT
and with larger horizons
H
(as expected from Lemma
4.2
, and from knowledge of how
RS
-
BC
works).
